\frac{1}{2n}\inf_{M}R{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\leq u_{i}\leq\frac{1}{2n}\sup_{M}R{\left(g,\varphi\right)}.

Therefore, there is a subsequence, also denoted
u_{i}
, which converges locally uniformly in
C^{\ell,\alpha}{\left(M\right)}
to a function
u
, which is defined on all of
M
and is still a solution to (
3.20
). Moreover, by Lemma
3.22
,
\left|u\right|
is bounded by an
L^{2}_{\varphi}
-integrable barrier, which decays like
e^{-\beta r}
as
r\rightarrow\infty
.

Set
\omega_{i}:=e^{-\frac{u_{i}}{2}}-1
and
\omega:=e^{-\frac{u}{2}}-1
. By Proposition
3.19
,
\omega_{i}
is the unique minimiser of the entropy over
\Omega_{i}
:

\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g},\Omega_{i}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}=\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g},\Omega_{i}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega_{i}\right)}.

By domain monotonicity of the entropy, we have

\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g},\Omega_{i}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\geq\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g},\Omega_{i+1}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}.

If we extend each
\omega_{i}
trivially outside
\Omega_{i}
, we get from the proof of Lemma
3.17
, that
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g},\Omega_{i}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega_{i}\right)}=\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega_{i}\right)}
is given by

\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega_{i}\right)}=\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}4\left|d\omega_{i}\right|^{2}+H\left(\omega_{i}\right)+R{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\left(\omega_{i}^{2}+2\omega_{i}\right)\;dV_{\varphi}+C(g,\varphi),

with
H,R,
and
C
as in said proof. As
H
has subextremal growth, in the sense that
H{\left(\omega\right)}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\omega^{2+\varepsilon}\right)}
where
\varepsilon
may be chosen so small that
H^{1}_{\varphi}\subset L^{2+\varepsilon}_{\varphi}
, and
\bigcup^{\infty}_{i=1}\Omega_{i}=M
, we see that

\lim\limits_{i\rightarrow\infty}\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g},\Omega_{i}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}=\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}.

From the coercivity estimate of Proposition
3.17
, we obtain a uniform bound on the
H^{1}_{\varphi}
-norms of the sequence
\omega_{i}
. By passing to a subsequence, if necessary, we may assume
\omega_{i}\rightharpoonup\omega
in
H^{1}_{\varphi}
. Using the weak lower semi-continuity of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}
, ensured by the upper bound in Proposition
3.17
, we have

\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\leq\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega\right)}\leq\liminf_{i\to\infty}\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega_{i}\right)}=\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}.

The barrier constructed in Lemma
3.22
can be used in conjunction with the dominated convergence theorem, to show that

\int_{M}H(\omega_{i})+R(g,\varphi){\left(\omega_{i}^{2}+2\omega_{i}\right)}\;dV_{\varphi}\longrightarrow\int_{M}H(\omega)+R(g,\varphi){\left(\omega^{2}+2\omega\right)}\;dV_{\varphi}.

Combined with the convergence of
\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{AH,\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega_{i}\right)}
, we actually have norm convergence
\left|\left|\omega_{i}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\varphi}}\to\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\varphi}}
, from which follows strong convergence. Concludingly, we have found a global
H^{1}_{\varphi}
-minimiser
\omega
with

\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}=\widetilde{\mathcal{W}}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi,\omega\right)}=\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi,f\right)},

where
f=\varphi-2\ln{\left(1+\omega\right)}
. The Euler–Lagrange equation (
3.20
) for
\omega
reads

\Delta_{\varphi}\omega+n\omega=n\widetilde{H}{\left(\omega\right)}-\frac{1}{4}{\left(\omega+1\right)}R{\left(g,\varphi\right)},

(3.26)

where

\widetilde{H}(\omega)=\omega-{\left(\omega+1\right)}\ln{\left(\omega+1\right)}=\mathcal{O}{\left(\omega^{2}\right)}.

Since the right hand side of (
3.26
) lies in
H^{1}_{\varphi}\cap C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta}
, we can apply Proposition
2.27
to see that
\omega\in H^{3}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
.

We now turn to proving the regularity statements for
f
and
f-\varphi
. This will be achieved by proving the same regularity statements for
\omega=e^{-\frac{1}{2}{\left(f-\varphi\right)}}-1
. Assuming
g
is of class
C^{\ell,\alpha}
, we have
R{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in C^{\ell-2,\alpha}{\left(M\right)}
and
\Delta_{\varphi}+n:C^{m,\alpha}{\left(M\right)}\rightarrow C^{m-2,\alpha}{\left(M\right)}
is an isomorphism by Proposition
2.27
.

Using (
3.26
), we may compute

\displaystyle\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|_{C^{m,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}\omega\right|\right|_{C^{m-2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle=C\Big|\Big|n\widetilde{H}{\left(\omega\right)}-\frac{1}{4}R{\left(g,\varphi\right)}{\left(\omega+1\right)}\Big|\Big|_{C^{m-2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C^{\prime}{\left(\big|\big|\widetilde{H}{\left(\omega\right)}\big|\big|_{C^{m-2,\alpha}}+\left|\left|R{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\right|\right|_{C^{m-2,\alpha}}{\left(\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|_{C^{m-2,\alpha}}+1\right)}\right)}

The right hand side is certainly finite for
m=2
, indeed it is even finite with respect to
C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta}
, as previously explained. Using that
C^{m,\alpha}{\left(M\right)}
is an algebra for
1\leq m\leq\ell
(see e.g.
[
Lee
, Lemma 3.6(a)]
), we find that
\widetilde{H}(\omega)\sim\omega^{2}
inherits the Hölder regularity of
\omega
. A bootstrapping argument then shows that
\omega\in C^{\ell,\alpha}
.

If we additionally assume that
g\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
(or
\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\varphi}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
), we gain
R(g,\varphi)\in C^{\ell-2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
(respectively
H^{\ell-2}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}
). Now the same bootstrapping argument goes through, using that
\Delta_{\varphi}+n:C^{m,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}\to C^{m-2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
(respectively
H^{m}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}\to H^{m-2}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}
) is an isomorphism for
m=2,\ldots,\ell
.
∎

We conclude this section by proving that the minimiser depends analytically on the pair
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
.

Proposition 3.27
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple,
\beta\in\left(\frac{n}{2},n\right)
and
\ell\geq 2
. Then the map

\mathcal{R}^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\ni{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\mapsto f_{g}-\varphi\in C^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}

is analytic, hence so is the map

\mathcal{R}^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\ni{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\mapsto\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}.

Moreover, the map

\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\ni{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\mapsto f_{g}-\varphi\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M\right)}

is analytic for
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
. This map should be understood simply as sending the pair
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
to a solution of (
3.20
), disregarding any minimising properties.

Proof.

First, consider the mapping

\Phi{\left(g,\varphi,f\right)}:=2\Delta f+\left|df\right|^{2}+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}+2nf-2n\varphi-\mathrm{scal}-n(n-1)-2n.

The differential of
\Phi
in the third argument is

D_{g,\varphi,f}\Phi{\left(0,0,v\right)}=2\Delta v+2\left\langle df,dv\right\rangle+2nv.

If we can show that

P_{g,\varphi,f}:=D_{g,\varphi,f}\Phi{\left(0,0,\cdot\right)}:C^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}\rightarrow C^{\ell-2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}

is an isomorphism, then we can deduce the claimed result by applying the implicit function theorem.

To this end, one can use an integration by parts argument with respect to the weighted volume measure
e^{-f}dV_{g}
to deduce that
P_{g,\varphi,f}
has trivial kernel. Indeed, if
v\in\ker P_{g,\varphi,f}
then

0=\int_{M}vP_{g,\varphi,f}v\;dV_{f}=2n\left|\left|v\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{f}{\left(M\right)}}^{2}-\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}\left\langle\nabla v^{2},\nu\right\rangle\;dA_{f},

with the boundary integral vanishing, as
v\in C_{\beta}^{\ell,\alpha}{\left(M\right)}
.

Next, setting
u:=f-\varphi
, we note that
P_{g,\varphi,f}=e^{\frac{u}{2}}\circ Q_{g,\varphi,f}\circ e^{-\frac{u}{2}}
, where

Q_{g,\varphi,f}{\left(v\right)}=2\Delta v+2nv+{\left(\Delta u+\frac{1}{2}\left|du\right|^{2}\right)}v.

By Proposition
2.27
, we know that

2\Delta+2n:C^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}\rightarrow C^{\ell-2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}

is an isomorphism for
\beta\in{\left(\frac{n}{2},n\right)}
. Moreover, since
u\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}(M)
by Theorem
3.25
, the operator
Q_{g,\varphi,f}
has the same indicial radius as
2\Delta+2n
, hence it is also Fredholm with zero index. Finally, since multiplication by
e^{\pm\frac{u}{2}}
is an isomorphism,
P_{g,\varphi,f}
is similarly Fredholm of index zero. We already know
P_{g,\varphi,f}
has trivial kernel, therefore it must be an isomorphism and we can apply the implicit function theorem, as desired. That this implies the entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
depends analytically on
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
is due to all the operations involved in the definition of the entropy being analytic.

The proof of the analogous result for Sobolev spaces is the same after replacing occurrences of
C^{\ell,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
and
C^{\ell-2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
with, respectively,
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M\right)}
and
H^{\ell-2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M\right)}
and using Lemma
2.14
to ensure
u
and its first two derivatives decay appropriately.
∎

In light of Proposition
3.27
, we should really write
f_{g,\varphi}
(or
f_{\mathfrak{g}}
) to reflect the dependence of
f
on
g
and
\varphi
. However, to simplify notation (and since
\varphi
itself is somewhat dependent on
g
), we shall usually write
f_{g}
, unless we are working with auxiliary metrics or we really want to emphasise the dependence on
\varphi
.

4
The First and Second Variations of the Entropy

Having proved the entropy is well-defined and finite in the previous section, we now compute its first and second variations. This will yield the diffeomorphism invariance of the entropy as well as our stability operator.

To make notation less cumbersome moving forward, we will fix a background static pair
\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)
and omit them when we write the entropy, instead writing just
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
. Additionally, we will at times use
D_{g,\varphi}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
to denote the first variation of the entropy, while
\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
will denote the corresponding (weighted)
L^{2}
-gradient (that is, the integrand of
D_{g,\varphi}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
). We will also analogously use
D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
to denote the variation of the entropy with the auxiliary metric
\mathfrak{g}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
as the base point, while
D^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
and
\nabla^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
(with appropriate subscripts) will be used to, respectively, denote the second variation of the entropy and the weighted
L^{2}
-Hessian of the entropy (that is, the integrand of
D^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
).

4.1
The First Variation

Proposition 4.1

(First variation)
.

Let
\frac{n}{2}<\beta<n
,
g\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
,
\varphi
be an approximate potential, and
\left|h\right|,\psi,v,f-\varphi\in C_{\beta}^{2,\alpha}{\left(M\right)}
. Then, for
\left|s\right|\ll 1
,

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g+sh,\varphi+s\psi,f+sv\right)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{g}+ng+\nabla^{2}f-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi,h\right\rangle\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{M}{\left(\Delta\varphi+\left\langle d\varphi,df\right\rangle-n\right)}\psi\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle-\int_{M}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g}f-v\right)}\EL f\;dV_{f},

where
\EL
is the Euler-Lagrange operator from (
3.9
).

Proof.

Recall from Definition
3.3
, that

\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}(g,\varphi,f)=\lim\limits_{R\rightarrow\infty}\left[\mathcal{W}_{R}(g,\varphi,f)-\frac{1}{2n}m_{\widehat{g},R}{\left(g-\widehat{g},\varphi\right)}+\mathcal{B}_{R}(g,\varphi,f)\right],

where

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{R}(g,\varphi,f)={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2n}\int_{B_{R}}\left|df\right|^{2}-\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}-2n(f-\varphi)+\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n(n-1)\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\int_{B_{R}}\Delta f+\left|df\right|^{2}-n-n\EL f\;dV_{f},

\displaystyle m_{\widehat{g},R}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}={}

\displaystyle-\int_{\partial B_{R}}\left\langle\delta_{\widehat{g}}h+d\tr_{\widehat{g}}h,\widehat{\nu}\right\rangle_{\widehat{g}}+\tr_{\widehat{g}}h\;\widehat{\nu}{\left(\varphi\right)}-h{\left(d\varphi,\widehat{\nu}\right)}\;d\widehat{A}_{\varphi},

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{R}(g,\varphi,f)={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\partial B_{R}}{\left(f-\varphi\right)}\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}\;dA_{\varphi}.

Using (
A.3
) and (
A.5
), we obtain the formula for the change in
\mathcal{W}_{R}
, with respect to an infinitesimal perturbation of the metric:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathcal{W}_{R}{\left(g+sh,\varphi,f\right)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{B_{R}}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{g},h\right\rangle+h{\left(df,df\right)}-h{\left(d\varphi,d\varphi\right)}-\Delta\tr h-\delta{\left(\delta h\right)}\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2n}\int_{B_{R}}\tr h{\left(\Delta f+\left|df\right|^{2}-n-n\EL f\right)}\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{B_{R}}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{g}+ng+\nabla^{2}f-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi,h\right\rangle\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}\int_{B_{R}}\tr h\>\EL f\;dV_{f}+\frac{1}{2n}m_{g,R}{\left(h,f\right)}.

Note that the second equality follows from integrating by parts. Similarly, for a perturbation of the potential
\varphi
, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathcal{W}_{R}{\left(g,\varphi+s\psi,f\right)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{B_{R}}\left\langle d\varphi,d\psi\right\rangle-n\psi\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{B_{R}}{\left(\Delta\varphi+\left\langle d\varphi,df\right\rangle-n\right)}\psi\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\partial B_{R}}\psi\>\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}\;dA_{f}.

where the second equality follows from integrating by parts. Finally, for a perturbation of
f
, Proposition
3.8
tells us that

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathcal{W}_{R}{\left(g,\varphi,f+sv\right)}={}

\displaystyle\int_{B_{R}}v\EL f\;dV_{f}+\frac{1}{n}\int_{\partial B_{R}}v\>\nu(f)\;dA_{f}.

Now we consider the variation of the mass and boundary terms. The variation of the mass is the following:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}m_{\widehat{g},R}{\left(g-\widehat{g}+sh,\varphi+s\psi\right)}

\displaystyle=m_{\widehat{g},R}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}-\int_{\partial B_{R}}

\displaystyle\biggl[\tr_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g-\widehat{g}\right)}\widehat{\nu}{\left(\psi\right)}-{\left(g-\widehat{g}\right)}{\left(d\psi,\widehat{\nu}\right)}

\displaystyle+\psi\left<\delta_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g-\widehat{g}\right)}+d\tr_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g-\widehat{g}\right)},\widehat{\nu}\right>_{\widehat{g}}

\displaystyle+\psi\tr_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g-\widehat{g}\right)}\widehat{\nu}{\left(\varphi\right)}-\psi{\left(g-\widehat{g}\right)}{\left(d\varphi,\widehat{\nu}\right)}\biggr]\;d\widehat{A}_{\varphi}

Note that only the mass term survives as
R\rightarrow\infty
, since
g-\widehat{g}\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
and
\psi\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
for
\beta>\frac{n}{2}
. As for the variation of the boundary term
\mathcal{B}_{R}
, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}_{R}{\left(g+sh,\varphi+s\psi,f+sv\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{n}\int_{\partial B_{R}}{\left(v-\psi\right)}\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}+{\left(f-\varphi\right)}\nu{\left(\psi\right)}+{\left(\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g^{T}}h-\psi\right)}{\left(f-\varphi\right)}\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}\;dA_{\varphi},

where
g^{T}=g|_{\partial B_{R}}
is the induced metric.
Since
f-\varphi,\psi\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
, only the first term survives as
R\rightarrow\infty
.

Putting what we have so far together yields

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}{\left(g+sh,\varphi+s\psi,f+sv\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{g}+ng+\nabla^{2}f-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi,h\right\rangle\;dV_{f}-\frac{1}{n}\int_{M}{\left(\Delta\varphi+\left\langle d\varphi,df\right\rangle-n\right)}\psi\;dV_{f}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{n}\lim_{R\to\infty}{\left(\int_{\partial B_{R}}v\>\nu{\left(f\right)}-\psi\>\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}\;dA_{f}-\int_{\partial B_{R}}{\left(v-\psi\right)}\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}\;dA_{\varphi}\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2n}m_{g}{\left(h,f\right)}-\frac{1}{2n}m_{\widehat{g}}(h,\varphi)-\int_{M}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g}f-v\right)}\EL f\;dV_{f}.

Since
dA_{f}={\left(1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-\beta r})\right)}dA_{\varphi}
and
f-\varphi,v,\psi=\mathcal{O}(e^{-\beta r})
, we may combine the boundary integrals:

\displaystyle\lim_{R\to\infty}{\left(\int_{\partial B_{R}}v\>\nu{\left(f\right)}-\psi\>\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}\;dA_{f}-\int_{\partial B_{R}}{\left(v-\psi\right)}\nu{\left(\varphi\right)}\;dA_{\varphi}\right)}

\displaystyle\qquad=\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}v\>\nu{\left(f-\varphi\right)}\;dA_{\varphi}

\displaystyle\qquad=0.

Applying the same principles, and
\left|h\right|,\left|g-\widehat{g}\right|,f-\varphi=\mathcal{O}{\left(e^{-\beta r}\right)}
, it is not hard to see that

m_{g}{\left(h,f\right)}-m_{\widehat{g}}(h,\varphi)=0.

The first variation formula now follows.
∎

For
\left|t\right|\ll 1
, let
g(t):=g+th
(with
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
) be a family of AH metrics with approximate potentials
\varphi(t):=\varphi+t\psi+\mathcal{O}{\left(t^{2}\right)}
and associated minimisers
f_{g(t)}:=f_{g}+tv+\mathcal{O}{\left(t^{2}\right)}
(with
v,\psi\in C^{\infty}{\left(M\right)}
). Note that, by Proposition
3.27
and
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
, we also have
v\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
. This implies
\psi\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
as
v-\psi\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
. We may therefore use Proposition
4.1
to conclude the following:

Corollary 4.2
.

Let
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
be the renormalised expander entropy of Definition
3.7
, and let
f_{g}
be the unique minimiser from Theorem
3.25
. The
L^{2}_{f_{g}}
-gradient of
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
is given by

\displaystyle\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}={\left(-\frac{1}{4n}{\left(2\mathrm{Ric}_{g}+2ng-2\>d\varphi\otimes d\varphi+\mathscr{L}_{\nabla f_{g}}g\right)},-\frac{1}{n}{\left(\Delta\varphi+\mathscr{L}_{\nabla f_{g}}\varphi-n\right)}\right)}.

(4.3)

The gradient flow is thus

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}g(t)&=-2\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{g(t)}+ng(t)-d\varphi(t)\otimes d\varphi(t)+\nabla^{2}f_{g(t)}\right)\\
\partial_{t}\varphi(t)&=-\left(\Delta\varphi(t)+\left<d\varphi(t),df_{g(t)}\right>-n\right).\end{cases}

(4.4)

With respect to the auxiliary metric,
\mathfrak{g}=e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+g
, the first variation in the direction
\mathfrak{h}=-2\psi e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+h
can be expressed as

D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(f_{g}-\varphi\right)},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}\;dV_{f_{g}}

(4.5)

Proof.

The gradient expression for
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
follows directly from Proposition
4.1
, with the only difference being
\EL f_{g}=0
. The gradient flow is then read off using
\mathscr{L}_{\nabla f_{g}}g=2\delta^{*}\nabla f_{g}=2\nabla^{2}f_{g}
and
\mathscr{L}_{\nabla f_{g}}\varphi=\left\langle d\varphi,df_{g}\right\rangle
.

The formula for the first variation with respect to the auxiliary metric is a reformulation of the gradient above, using only (
2.15
) and
\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}f=-\left\langle d\varphi,df\right\rangle_{g}e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+\nabla^{2}_{g}f
, for any
\tau
-independent function
f
on
M
.
∎

Remark 4.6
.

Modulo the diffeomorphisms generated by
\nabla{\left(f_{g}-\varphi\right)}
, the gradient flow is given by

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}g(t)=-2{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{g(t)}+ng(t)-d\varphi(t)\otimes d\varphi(t)+\nabla^{2}\varphi(t)\right)}\\
\partial_{t}\varphi(t)=-{\left(\Delta\varphi(t)+\left|d\varphi(t)\right|^{2}-n\right)}.\end{cases}

Note that these are precisely the equations of staticity (
2.10
).

Though the entropy is ostensibly only defined for pairs
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
, it is actually possible to extend the concept to an
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
neighbourhood of
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
. As stated back in Proposition
3.27
, the map sending
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
to
u_{g}=f_{g}-\varphi\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
is analytic. Here
u_{g}
should a priori not be thought of as a minimiser, but simply a solution to the Euler–Lagrange equation (
3.20
). It was not possible back then to conclude that the entropy itself was well-defined and analytic as a consequence, but it is now.

Corollary 4.7
.

The map

\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\ni{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\longmapsto\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}

is well-defined and analytic.

Proof.

By
[
Lee
, Lemma 4.9]
, we can approximate
\mathfrak{g}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
by a sequence
\mathfrak{g}_{i}={\left(g_{i},\varphi_{i}\right)}
such that
\mathfrak{g}_{i}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\in C_{c}^{\infty}
. Clearly, every
\mathfrak{g}_{i}
satisfies the decay conditions necessary for
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{i}\right)}
to be well-defined and finite, and for the first variation formula (
4.5
) to hold. Thus, through the fundamental theorem of calculus, we get

\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{i}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{j}\right)}\right|\leq\sup_{t\in[0,1]}\left|{\left(\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)\right)},\mathfrak{g}_{i}-\mathfrak{g}_{j}\right)}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)}}\right|,

where
\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)=\mathfrak{g}_{i}+t{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{j}-\mathfrak{g}_{i}\right)}
, and

\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}=-\frac{1}{2n}{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

For
i,j
sufficiently large, we may assume that
\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}\leq C
(since
\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)\overset{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}{\longrightarrow}\mathfrak{g}
for
i,j\to\infty
and all
t\in[0,1]
). Since
\ell\geq\frac{n}{2}+2\geq 2
, we also obtain a uniform bound on the gradient

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)\right)}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell-2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C^{\prime}\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{ij}(t)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C^{\prime\prime}.

Thus, Cauchy-Schwarz gives

\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{i}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{j}\right)}\right|\leq C^{\prime\prime}\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{i}-\mathfrak{g}_{j}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

where we also used that the
L^{2}
-norms are comparable for all metrics involved. This proves that
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{i}\right)}
is a Cauchy sequence, converging to some finite value, which we denote by
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}
. Additionally, it is clear from the explicit expression of the gradient that it carries over to the
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
setting. Now that we know that the entropy is well-defined, we can use the fact that

\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\ni\mathfrak{g}\longmapsto\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}\in H^{\ell-2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}(M)

is analytic to conclude that

\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}=\int_{0}^{1}{\left(\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}+t{\left(\mathfrak{g}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)},\mathfrak{g}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}}\;dt

is also analytic (as it is composed of analytic maps).
∎

In the remainder of this section,
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
is assumed to be in either
\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
(with
\frac{n}{2}<\beta<n
) or
\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
(with
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
). The same goes for the flow
{\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)}
in Lemma
4.13
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Suppose
(g,\varphi)
is a critical point for the renormalised expander entropy, then
(g,\varphi)
is a static pair.

Proof.

Criticality implies

\begin{cases}\mathrm{Ric}_{g}+ng-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi+\nabla^{2}f&=0\\
\Delta\varphi+\left\langle d\varphi,df\right\rangle-n&=0,\end{cases}

(4.9)

where
f=f_{g}
is the unique minimiser and solution to the Euler–Lagrange equation:

2\Delta f+\left|df\right|^{2}+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}+2n(f-\varphi)-{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n(n-1)\right)}-2n=0.

Taking the trace of the first equation of (
4.9
), we obtain

\Delta f+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}-n=\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n(n-1).

By inserting this in the Euler–Lagrange equation, it simplifies to

\Delta f+\left|df\right|^{2}-n+2n(f-\varphi)=0.

Subtracting the second equation of (
4.9
) from this, leaves us with

\Delta(f-\varphi)+\left\langle df,d(f-\varphi)\right\rangle+2n(f-\varphi)=0.

Multiplying the above by
(f-\varphi)
and integrating over the ball
B_{R}
with respect to the weighted volume
e^{-f}dV
yields

0=\int_{B_{R}}\left|d(f-\varphi)\right|^{2}+2n\left|f-\varphi\right|^{2}\;dV_{f}-\int_{\partial B_{R}}(f-\varphi)\nu{\left(f-\varphi\right)}\;dA_{f}.

Taking the limit as
R\to\infty
, and using that
{\left(f-\varphi\right)}=\mathcal{O}_{1}{\left(e^{-\beta r}\right)}
, we obtain

\left|\left|d{\left(f-\varphi\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{f}{\left(M\right)}}^{2}+2n\left|\left|f-\varphi\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{f}{\left(M\right)}}^{2}=0.

This proves that
f=\varphi
, which turns (
4.9
) into the defining equations for a static pair (
2.10
).
∎

Note that Proposition
4.8
tells us that no non-trivial solitons are possible.

Remark 4.10
.

While static metrics are the only true critical points for the entropy, another class of semi-critical metrics will play an important role in the proof of the local positive mass result (Theorem
5.14
). Let
\mathfrak{g}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
be a pair satisfying

\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n(n-1)\qquad\qquad\&\qquad\qquad\Delta\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}=n.

This means both
g
and
\mathfrak{g}
are constant scalar curvature (CSC) metrics. If
\mathfrak{h}=-2\psi e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+\eta g
preserves the conformal class of the base metric
g
, then

\displaystyle D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\mathfrak{h})={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi n}\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{4\pi n}\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\eta{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{g}-\Delta\varphi-\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}+n^{2}\right)}+2\psi{\left(\Delta\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}-n\right)}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 0,

where the only ingredients were the formula for the auxiliary Ricci curvature (
2.15
), and
f_{g}=\varphi
from Proposition
3.14
.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
X
be a vector field on
M
such that
\delta^{*}X\in C_{\beta}^{2,\alpha}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
(or
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
). Then

{\left(\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(g,\varphi),{\left(\mathscr{L}_{X}g,\mathscr{L}_{X}\varphi\right)}\right)}_{L^{2}_{f}{\left(M\right)}}=0.

In particular, the entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(g,\varphi)
is invariant under the action of diffeomorphisms that preserve the structure of the auxiliary metric
\mathfrak{g}=e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+g
.

Proof.

To shorten notation, we set

\displaystyle\nabla\mu_{1}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{2n}{\left(\mathrm{Ric}+ng+\nabla^{2}f-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi\right)},

\displaystyle\nabla\mu_{2}

\displaystyle:=-\frac{1}{n}{\left(\Delta\varphi+\left\langle df,d\varphi\right\rangle-n\right)},

where
f=f_{g}
is the unique solution to the Euler–Lagrange equation for the pair
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
. Let
\theta_{t}
be the flow of
X
. Then Proposition
4.1
tells us the first variation is

\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathcal{W}_{\mathrm{AH}}\left(\theta_{s}^{*}g,\theta_{s}^{*}\varphi,\theta_{s}^{*}f\right)=\int_{M}\left<\nabla\mu_{1},2\delta^{\ast}X\right>\;dV_{f}+\int_{M}\nabla\mu_{2}X\left(\varphi\right)\;dV_{f}.

(4.12)

Integrating by parts, we have

\displaystyle\int_{M}\left\langle\nabla\mu_{1},\delta^{*}X\right\rangle\;dV_{f}=\int_{M}\left\langle\delta\nabla\mu_{1}+\nabla\mu_{1}\cdot df,X\right\rangle\;dV_{f}.

There is no limiting boundary term as both
\nabla\mu_{1}
and
\left|X\right|
decay as
e^{-\beta r}
. By using the contracted second Bianchi identity,
\delta\mathrm{Ric}=-\frac{1}{2}d\mathrm{scal}
, and the Ricci identity,
\Delta\nabla f=\nabla\Delta f-\mathrm{Ric}\cdot df
, we set about simplifying the expression above:

\displaystyle\delta\nabla\mu_{1}+\nabla\mu_{1}\cdot df={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\left(\delta\mathrm{Ric}+\Delta\nabla f-\Delta\varphi\;d\varphi+\nabla^{2}\varphi\cdot d\varphi\right.

\displaystyle\left.+\mathrm{Ric}\cdot df+n\;df+\nabla^{2}f\cdot df-\left\langle d\varphi,df\right\rangle\;d\varphi\right)

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}d{\left(\Delta f+\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla f\right|^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla\varphi\right|^{2}+nf-n\varphi-\frac{1}{2}\mathrm{scal}\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2n}{\left(\Delta\varphi+\left\langle d\varphi,df\right\rangle-n\right)}\;d\varphi

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2}d{\left(\EL f\right)}-\frac{1}{2}\nabla\mu_{2}\;d\varphi.

Since
\EL f=0
, we have

\int_{M}\left\langle\nabla\mu_{1},\delta^{*}X\right\rangle\;dV_{f}=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\nabla\mu_{2}\left\langle\nabla\varphi,X\right\rangle\;dV_{f}.

Substituting this into (
4.12
) yields the desired result and completes the proof.
∎

The diffeomorphism invariance of the entropy now allows us to prove that the entropy is non-decreasing along the modified Ricci-harmonic flow (
4.4
).

Lemma 4.13
.

Let
\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)
be a solution of (
4.4
). Then the function
t\mapsto\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)}
is monotonically increasing. Moreover, it is strictly increasing unless
{\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)}
is a family of static pairs, in which case the entropy is constant in time.

Proof.

Proposition
4.11
tells us the following for the first variation of the entropy:

D_{g,\varphi}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}\left(\mathscr{L}_{\nabla f}g,\mathscr{L}_{\nabla f}\varphi\right)=0.

Then, since
\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)
solves (
4.4
), the first variation at any given
t
is

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=t}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g(s),\varphi(s)\right)}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{n}\int_{M}\left|\mathrm{Ric}\left(g(t)\right)+ng(t)+\nabla^{2}f_{g(t)}-d\varphi(t)\otimes d\varphi(t)\right|^{2}\;dV_{f_{g(t)}}

\displaystyle+\frac{2}{n}\int_{M}\left|\Delta\varphi(t)+\left\langle df_{g(t)},d\varphi(t)\right\rangle-n\right|^{2}\;dV_{f_{g(t)}}

\displaystyle\geq{}

\displaystyle 0.

The equality case follows from Proposition
4.8
.
∎

4.2
The Second Variation

Now we turn to deriving our stability operator through the computation of the second variation of the entropy. Before doing this, we make the following observation:

If we let
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
be a critical point of the renormalised expander entropy – that is to say, a static pair – then the associated Poincaré–Einstein metric
\mathfrak{g}=e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+g
provides a natural splitting of the space of variations of
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
, as given by (
2.21
).

For a general variation
\mathfrak{h}={\left(h,\psi\right)}
of
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
(see (
2.16
) for the notation), it is sometimes prudent to assume it is
transverse
(divergence free). The consequence for the pair
{\left(h,\psi\right)}
is provided by (
2.19
):

\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}=\delta_{g}h+h\cdot d\varphi+2\psi\;d\varphi=0.

(4.14)

If we instead assume
\mathfrak{h}
is trace free, we obtain another condition on the pair
(h,\psi)
using (
2.18
):

\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}=-2{\left(\psi-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g}h\right)}=0.

Now consider a family of triplets
{\left(g_{s},\varphi_{s},f_{s}\right)}
, where
f_{s}
is the unique solution to the Euler–Lagrange equation with respect to
\mathfrak{g}_{s}={\left(g_{s},\varphi_{s}\right)}
and
f_{0}=\varphi_{0}
. Then we may use (
3.9
), (
A.3
), (
A.7
), and (
A.8
), to linearise
\EL

0=\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}2n\EL_{\,g_{s},\varphi_{s}}\!f_{s}=2{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}+n\right)}{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g_{0}}h\right)}-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}\mathfrak{h}+\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}+n\mathfrak{g}_{0},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}_{0}},

(4.15)

where, as usual,
v=\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}f_{s}
. In the particular case that
\mathfrak{g}_{0}
is static and
\mathfrak{h}
is transverse, is simplifies to

0=2{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}+n\right)}{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g_{0}}h\right)},

(4.16)

which implies
v=\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g_{0}}h
.

It is now time to compute the second variation of
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
, here in the form of the Hessian. In the following
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}={\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
will be used to denote an arbitrary static pair, not necessarily the background pair of sections
3.1
-
4.1
.

Proposition 4.17

(Second Variation)
.

Let
(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi})
be a static triple, then the
L^{2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
–Hessian of the entropy is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\nabla^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(h,\psi\right)}&\\
=-\frac{1}{2n}&\biggl(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E,\widehat{\varphi}}h+{\left(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\otimes d\widehat{\varphi}+2\psi\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}+\nabla^{2}{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr h\right)}-\delta^{*}{\left(\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}\right)},\\
&\quad\quad\>2\Delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}\psi+2\left\langle h,\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle-2\left\langle\delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}h,d\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle+2\left\langle d{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr h\right)},d\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle\biggr),\end{split}

(4.18)

where
\Delta_{E,\widehat{\varphi}}h=\Delta_{E}h+\nabla_{\nabla\widehat{\varphi}}h=\nabla^{*}\nabla h-2\overset{\;\circ}{R}_{\widehat{g}}h+\nabla_{\nabla\widehat{\varphi}}h
and
\overset{\;\circ}{R}_{\widehat{g}}h:=\widehat{g}^{km}\widehat{g}^{\ell p}\widehat{R}_{ikj\ell}h_{mp}
.
Note that
\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}
can be interpreted as a 1-form on
M
due to (
2.19
).

Proof.

Observe that the terms in the following computation involving the variation of the weighted volume vanish after evaluating at a critical point, so we will omit them. Specifically, the Hessian is obtained as the entry–wise variation of the gradient (
4.3
).

Combining the variational formulas (
A.2
), (
A.6
), (
A.7
), (
A.8
) and evaluating them at the critical point
\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)
(so
\left.f_{g_{s}}\right|_{s=0}=\widehat{\varphi}
by Proposition
4.8
), we obtain

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}&{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{g_{s}}+\nabla^{2}_{g_{s}}f_{g_{s}}+ng_{s}-d\varphi_{s}\otimes d\varphi_{s}\right)}\\
={}&\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{L}h+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\nabla\widehat{\varphi}}h+nh+\frac{1}{2}h\times\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}+\nabla^{2}{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr h\right)}\\
&-\delta^{*}{\left(\delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}h\right)}-d\psi\otimes d\widehat{\varphi}-d\widehat{\varphi}\otimes d\psi,\end{split}

(4.19)

where
\left(h\times k\right)_{ij}=\widehat{g}^{\ell m}\left(h_{i\ell}k_{mj}+h_{j\ell}k_{mi}\right)
. Recalling the relationship between the auxiliary and shift divergence,
\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}=\delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}h+2\psi\;d\widehat{\varphi}
, the final line may be reduced to

-\delta^{*}{\left(\delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}h\right)}-d\psi\otimes d\widehat{\varphi}-d\widehat{\varphi}\otimes d\psi=-\delta^{*}{\left(\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}\right)}+2\psi\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}.

A further reduction is possible by using the relationship between the Lichnerowicz Laplacian and the Einstein operator:
\Delta_{L}h=\Delta_{E}h+h\times\mathrm{Ric}
. Since the static equations hold, we have

\displaystyle\begin{split}\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{L}h+nh+\frac{1}{2}h\times\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}={}&\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E}h+\frac{1}{2}h\times{\left(\mathrm{Ric}+n\widehat{g}+\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\\
={}&\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E}h+{\left(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\otimes d\widehat{\varphi}.\end{split}

(4.20)

The variation of the second term of the gradient of
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
, scaled by the appropriate factor of two, is

\displaystyle\begin{split}\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}&{\left(2\Delta_{g_{s}}\varphi_{s}+2\left\langle df_{g_{s}},d\varphi_{s}\right\rangle_{g_{s}}-2n\right)}\\
={}&2\Delta\psi+2\left\langle h,\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}-d\widehat{\varphi}\otimes d\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle\\
&-2\left\langle\delta h-d\psi,d\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle+2\left\langle d{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr h\right)},d\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle.\end{split}

(4.21)

The Hessian (
4.18
) now follows from the use of (
4.19
) and (
4.21
), when differentiating (
4.3
).
∎

Corollary 4.22
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple. If the variation
{\left(h,\psi\right)}
is transverse (in the sense of (
4.14
)), then

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}

\displaystyle{\left(h,\psi\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E,\widehat{\varphi}}h+{\left(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\otimes d\widehat{\varphi}+2\psi\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi},\;2\Delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}\psi+2\left\langle h,\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle+4\psi\left|d\widehat{\varphi}\right|^{2}\right).

Proof.

Follows immediately from Proposition
4.17
and the discussion at the start of this section.
∎

Proposition 4.23
.

Let
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static pair with auxiliary (PE) metric
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}=e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}\;d\tau^{2}+\widehat{g}
. If
\mathfrak{h}=-2\psi e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}\;d\tau^{2}+h
, then

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}={}

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E,\widehat{\varphi}}h+(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi})\otimes d\widehat{\varphi}+2\psi\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi},\;2\Delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}\psi+2\left\langle h,\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle+4\psi\left|d\widehat{\varphi}\right|^{2}\right)}.

In particular:

D^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=-\frac{1}{2n}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}+\nabla^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{\widehat{g}}h\right)}-\delta^{*}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{h}\right)}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

(4.24)

where
v
is the variation of the minimiser
f_{\widehat{g}}
and solves (
4.15
). In (
4.24
) integration is over
M
, but with respect to the volume element of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
– that is to say,
L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}(M):=L^{2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}(M)
.

Proof.

To simplify notation, we will use
g
for both
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\widehat{g}
, distinguishing them simply by their index. A similar approach is applied to
\mathfrak{h}
and
h
. Integers
i,j,m,l
will run from
1
to
n
, thus indexing only the coordinates on
M
. We commence by calculating the
ij
-components of
\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}
:

\displaystyle{\left(\nabla^{*}\nabla\right)}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}_{ij}={}

\displaystyle-g^{lm}\nabla_{l}{\left(\nabla h\right)}_{mij}-g^{\tau\tau}\nabla_{\tau}{\left(\nabla h\right)}_{\tau ij}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{*}\nabla h_{ij}+g^{\tau\tau}{\left(\Gamma_{\tau\tau}^{m}\nabla_{m}h_{ij}+\Gamma_{\tau i}^{\tau}\nabla_{\tau}h_{\tau j}+\Gamma_{\tau j}^{\tau}\nabla_{\tau}h_{i\tau}\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{*}\nabla h_{ij}+\nabla^{m}\widehat{\varphi}\nabla_{m}h_{ij}-e^{2\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(\Gamma_{\tau i}^{\tau}\Gamma_{\tau\tau}^{m}h_{mj}+2\Gamma_{\tau i}^{\tau}\Gamma_{\tau j}^{\tau}h_{\tau\tau}+\Gamma_{\tau j}^{\tau}\Gamma_{\tau\tau}^{m}h_{im}\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{*}\nabla h_{ij}+\nabla_{\nabla\widehat{\varphi}}h_{ij}+d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}g^{ml}d\widehat{\varphi}_{l}h_{mj}-2e^{2\widehat{\varphi}}d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j}h_{\tau\tau}+g^{ml}d\widehat{\varphi}_{l}h_{mi}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{*}\nabla h_{ij}+\nabla_{\nabla\widehat{\varphi}}h_{ij}+d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}{\left(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}_{j}+4\psi d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j}+{\left(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j},

\displaystyle(\overset{\;\circ}{R}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h})_{ij}={}

\displaystyle R_{i\;\;\;\;\;j}^{\;\;lm}h_{lm}+R_{i\;\;\;\;j}^{\;\ \tau\tau}h_{\tau\tau}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(\overset{\;\circ}{R}h)_{ij}-e^{2\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(\partial_{i}\Gamma_{\tau j}^{\tau}+\Gamma_{\tau i}^{\tau}\Gamma_{\tau j}^{\tau}-\Gamma_{ij}^{m}\Gamma_{\tau m}^{\tau}\right)}h_{\tau\tau}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(\overset{\;\circ}{R}h)_{ij}+e^{2\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(\nabla^{2}_{ij}\widehat{\varphi}-d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j}\right)}h_{\tau\tau}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle(\overset{\;\circ}{R}h)_{ij}-2\psi\nabla^{2}_{ij}\widehat{\varphi}+2\psi d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j}.

Which means we can express the
ij
-components of the Einstein operator as

\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}_{ij}=\Delta_{E}h_{ij}+\nabla_{\nabla\widehat{\varphi}}h_{ij}+d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}{\left(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}_{j}+{\left(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j}+4\psi\nabla^{2}_{ij}\widehat{\varphi}.

Next we calculate the
\tau\tau
-component of the Einstein operator

\displaystyle{\left(\nabla^{*}\nabla\right)}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}_{\tau\tau}={}

\displaystyle-g^{\tau\tau}\nabla_{\tau}{\left(\nabla h\right)}_{\tau\tau\tau}-g^{ij}\nabla_{i}{\left(\nabla h\right)}_{j\tau\tau}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle g^{\tau\tau}\Gamma_{\tau\tau}^{i}{\left(\partial_{i}h_{\tau\tau}-2\Gamma_{\tau\tau}^{k}h_{ik}-4\Gamma_{i\tau}^{\tau}h_{\tau\tau}\right)}

\displaystyle-g^{ij}{\left(\nabla^{2}_{ij}(h_{\tau\tau})+2h_{\tau\tau}\nabla^{2}_{ij}\widehat{\varphi}+2\Gamma_{i\tau}^{\tau}\Gamma_{j\tau}^{\tau}h_{\tau\tau}-4\Gamma_{i\tau}^{\tau}\partial_{j}h_{\tau\tau}\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}g^{ij}\partial_{j}\widehat{\varphi}{\left(2\partial_{j}\psi+2(h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi})_{i}+8\psi\partial_{i}\widehat{\varphi}\right)}

\displaystyle-g^{ij}{\left(\nabla^{2}_{ij}{\left(h_{\tau\tau}\right)}+2h_{\tau\tau}\nabla_{ij}^{2}\widehat{\varphi}+4\partial_{i}\widehat{\varphi}\partial_{j}(h_{\tau\tau})+4h_{\tau\tau}\partial_{i}\widehat{\varphi}\partial_{j}\widehat{\varphi}\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}g^{ij}{\left(2d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}d\psi_{j}+4\psi d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{j}+2d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}h_{jm}g^{ml}d\widehat{\varphi}_{l}-2\nabla^{2}_{ij}\psi\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(2\left\langle d\widehat{\varphi},d\psi\right\rangle+4\psi\left|d\widehat{\varphi}\right|^{2}+2h{\left(d\widehat{\varphi},d\widehat{\varphi}\right)}+2\Delta\psi\right)},

\displaystyle(\overset{\;\circ}{R}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h})_{\tau\tau}={}

\displaystyle g^{ij}g^{lm}R_{\tau il\tau}h_{jm}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle g^{ij}g^{lm}g_{\tau\tau}g_{\tau\tau}R_{i\;\;\;\;l}^{\;\;\tau\tau}h_{jm}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}g^{ij}g^{lm}{\left(\nabla^{2}_{il}\widehat{\varphi}-d\widehat{\varphi}_{i}d\widehat{\varphi}_{l}\right)}h_{jm}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi}-d\widehat{\varphi}\otimes d\widehat{\varphi},h\right\rangle.

These combine to produce

\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}_{\tau\tau}=-2e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(\Delta_{\widehat{\varphi}}\psi+2\psi\left|d\widehat{\varphi}\right|^{2}+\psi\left\langle\nabla^{2}\widehat{\varphi},h\right\rangle\right)},

which, together with the
ij
-components, gives the claimed formula for
\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}
. The expression (
4.24
) is the resulting simplification of (
4.18
), with the added use of

\nabla^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}f=-\left\langle df,d\widehat{\varphi}\right\rangle_{\widehat{g}}e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}\;d\tau^{2}+\nabla^{2}_{\widehat{g}}f

applied to
f=v-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{\widehat{g}}h
.
∎

Remark 4.25
.

As was remarked upon in the previous subsection (see
4.10
), metric pairs
\mathfrak{g}=(g,\varphi)
satisfying

\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n(n-1)\qquad\qquad\&\qquad\qquad\Delta\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}=n,

are critical points for the entropy restricted to directions preserving the conformal class of
g
.

An additional property of such a pair is the fact that the second variation in the direction of
\mathfrak{h}=-2\psi e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+\eta g
, is the same as that of static metrics: (
4.24
). The argument is simple, as the only time during the proof of the second variation we used the static equations was in (
4.20
). While this simplification does not hold outright for the non-static pair
(g,\varphi)
, it does hold “weakly” when
h=\eta g
:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left\langle\eta g\times{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{g}+ng+\nabla^{2}\varphi\right)},\eta g\right\rangle={}

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\tr{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{g}+ng+\nabla^{2}\varphi\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\eta^{2}{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n^{2}-\Delta\varphi\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\eta^{2}\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\left\langle{\left(\eta g\cdot d\varphi\right)}\otimes d\varphi,\eta g\right\rangle.

We end this section by estimating the second variation of the entropy along specific curves of auxiliary metrics and pairs
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
. The following proposition even adds a third type of semi-critical point for the entropy.

Proposition 4.26
.

1.

Let
\mathfrak{g}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
be a constant scalar curvature metric,
\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}}=-n(n+1)
, then it is a critical point for the entropy restricted to its conformal class, and

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left({\left(1+s\chi\right)}\mathfrak{g}\right)}\leq-\frac{1}{4n}\left|\left|\chi\mathfrak{g}\right|\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

for every
\chi\in H^{\ell}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}=H^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(M\right)}
.

2.

Let
(g,\varphi)
be a pair, such that
\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n(n-1)
and
\Delta\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}=n
. Then
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
is a critical point for the entropy restricted to variations preserving the conformal class of
g
. Furthermore, for every
\eta,\psi\in H^{\ell}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}
,

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left({\left(1+s\eta\right)}g,\varphi+s\psi\right)}\leq-C_{g,\varphi}\left|\left|{\left(\eta g,\psi\right)}\right|\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

(4.27)

for some
C_{g,\varphi}>0
.

Proof.

(1.)
Consider the curve of auxiliary metrics
\mathfrak{g}_{s}=(1+s\chi)\mathfrak{g}
. Recall that Proposition
3.14
says that the minimiser
f_{s}
agrees with
\varphi_{s}
at
s=0
. Thus, from Corollary
4.2
, we see that
\mathfrak{g}
is conformally critical:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left((1+s\chi)\mathfrak{g}\right)}={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(f-\varphi\right)},\chi\mathfrak{g}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n(n+1)\right)}\chi\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 0,

where, as we usually do, view
dV_{\mathfrak{g}}=dV_{\varphi}
as a volume form on
M
.

Using that
\mathfrak{g}
is CSC and that
f_{0}=f_{\mathfrak{g}}=\varphi
, it is a simple task to check that the second variation in a conformal direction springs from the first variation of the
L^{2}
-gradient:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left((1+s\chi)\mathfrak{g}\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\left\langle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{s}}+n\mathfrak{g}_{s}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{s}}{\left(f_{s}-\varphi_{s}\right)}\right)},\chi\mathfrak{g}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\left\langle\frac{1}{2}{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\chi\right)}\mathfrak{g}-\frac{n-1}{2}\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\chi+n\chi\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(f^{\prime}-\frac{\chi}{2}\right)},\chi\mathfrak{g}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\frac{n+1}{2}\chi\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\chi+n(n+1)\chi^{2}-\chi\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(f^{\prime}-\frac{n\chi}{2}\right)}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}.

The form of the final term of the integrand is designed to allow for the use of the linearised Euler–Lagrange equation (
4.15
):

f^{\prime}-\frac{n\chi}{2}=\frac{1}{2}{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}^{-1}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\chi\mathfrak{g}\right)}=-\frac{1}{2}{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}^{-1}\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\chi.

Since
{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}^{-1}
is a positive operator, we have:

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left((1+s\chi)\mathfrak{g}\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\frac{n+1}{2}\left|\nabla\chi\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n(n+1)\chi^{2}+\frac{1}{2}{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\chi\right)}{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}^{-1}{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\chi\right)}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\frac{n+1}{2}\left|\nabla\chi\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n(n+1)\chi^{2}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle-\frac{1}{2n}\int_{M}\frac{1}{2}\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\chi\mathfrak{g}\right)}\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\left|\chi\mathfrak{g}\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}.

The inequality in first part of proposition is then obtained by using
n\geq 3>\frac{1}{2}
.

(2.)
Let
(g,\varphi)
be a pair as in the statement of the proposition and consider a perturbation of the auxiliary metric
\mathfrak{g}=e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+g
, of the form
\mathfrak{h}=-2\psi e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+\eta g
. It has already been proven that
(g,\varphi)
is critical in this direction (see Remark
4.10
). Before we can show (
4.27
), we prove two claims.

Claim 4.28
.

The variation
\mathfrak{h}={\left(h,\psi\right)}
splits uniquely as
\mathfrak{h}=\mathfrak{y}+\chi\mathfrak{g}
, with
D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathrm{scal}{\left(\mathfrak{y}\right)}=0
and
\chi\in H^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{g}}(M)
. Furthermore,
\mathfrak{y}
integrates to a curve of CSC metrics. That is, we find a curve of metrics
\mathfrak{g}_{s}=e^{-2\varphi_{s}}\;d\tau^{2}+g_{s}
with
\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}_{s}}=-n(n+1)
and
\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathfrak{g}_{s}=\mathfrak{y}
.

Proof.

To prove the claim, note first that

D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathrm{scal}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}={\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},

where we used (
2.15
) to compute that

\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}=2n\psi+{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{g}-n\right)}\eta=-n{\left(n\eta-2\psi\right)}=-n\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}.

Now the claimed decomposition of
\mathfrak{h}
is equivalent to the equation

\displaystyle{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}=n(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n+1)\chi,

which is uniquely solvable for a function
\chi\in H^{\ell}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}\subset H^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\mathfrak{M}\right)}
, provided that
\mathfrak{h}\in H^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(S^{2}\mathfrak{M}\right)}
. This yields the claimed decomposition.

Next, we observe that
D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathrm{scal}
is surjective on this class of perturbations. Therefore, the set

\mathcal{A}:=\left\{\mathfrak{g}=e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+g\,:\;\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}}=-n(n+1)\right\}

is a smooth manifold satisfying
T_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathcal{A}=\mathrm{ker}(D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathrm{scal})
. This establishes the second part of the claim.
∎

Next, we claim the following:

Claim 4.29
.

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d^{2}}{drds}\right|_{r,s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}+s\chi\mathfrak{g}+r\mathfrak{y}\right)}=0

for every
\mathfrak{y}\in T_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathcal{A}
and
\chi\in H^{\ell}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}\subset H^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{g}}(\mathfrak{M})
.

Proof.

By the previous claim, and because
\mathfrak{g}
is a semi-critical metric, we can replace
\mathfrak{g}+s\chi\mathfrak{g}+r\mathfrak{y}
by the
2
-parameter family
(1+s\chi)\mathfrak{g}_{r}
, where
\mathfrak{g}_{r}
is a curve in
\mathcal{A}
with
\left.\frac{d}{dr}\right|_{r=0}\mathfrak{g}_{r}=\mathfrak{y}
. By the first part of the proposition,

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{r}+s\chi\mathfrak{g}_{r}\right)}=0

for all
r
, and differentiating this identity with respect to
r
yields

\displaystyle 0=\left.\frac{d^{2}}{drds}\right|_{r,s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{r}+s\chi\mathfrak{g}_{r}\right)}=\left.\frac{d^{2}}{drds}\right|_{r,s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}+s\chi\mathfrak{g}+r\mathfrak{y}\right)},

as desired.
∎

Thus, the second variation is orthogonal with respect to the splitting
H^{\ell}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}\cdot\mathfrak{g}\oplus T_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathcal{A}
. For
\mathfrak{h}=\chi\mathfrak{g}\in H^{\ell}_{\varphi}{\left(M\right)}\cdot\mathfrak{g}
, the desired estimate follows already from the first part of the proposition. It therefore suffices to establish the claimed estimate for tensors
\mathfrak{h}\in T_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathcal{A}
. That is, for those tensors
\mathfrak{h}
which satisfy

0=D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathrm{scal}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}={\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}.

(4.30)

When combined with the linearised Euler–Lagrange equation (
4.15
), scalar curvature preservation is revealed to be equivalent to
\psi=v
, as

\displaystyle 0={}

\displaystyle 2{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}{\left(v-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g}h\right)}-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 2{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}{\left(v-\psi-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right)}-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle 2{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}{\left(v-\psi\right)},

and
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n
has trivial kernel. Using this and (
2.18
), we find that (
4.24
) becomes

\displaystyle D^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=-\frac{1}{4n}{\left(\Delta_{E,\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}-\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}-2\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{h}\right)}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

Recall that (
4.24
) holds for the non-static pair
(g,\varphi)
by Remark
4.25
. The explicit form of
\mathfrak{h}
may be used to compute the curvature term in the definition of the Einstein operator:

\displaystyle\overset{\;\circ}{R}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}

\displaystyle=\overset{\;\circ}{R}_{g}\left(\eta g\right)-2\psi\left(\nabla^{2}\varphi-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi\right)+e^{-2\varphi}\left<\nabla^{2}\varphi-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi,\eta g\right>d\tau^{2}

\displaystyle=\eta\mathrm{Ric}_{g}-2\psi\left(\nabla^{2}\varphi-d\varphi\otimes d\varphi\right)-\eta e^{-2\varphi}\left(\Delta\varphi-\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}\right)d\tau^{2}

\displaystyle=\eta\mathrm{Ric}_{g}-2\psi{\left(\nabla^{2}_{g}\varphi+d\varphi\otimes d\varphi\right)}-n\eta e^{-2\varphi}d\tau^{2},

which means

\displaystyle\left\langle\overset{\;\circ}{R}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}=\eta^{2}\mathrm{scal}_{g}+2\psi\eta{\left(\Delta_{g}\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}\right)}+2n\psi\eta=\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}-\left|\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}},

where we used

\displaystyle\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle=4\psi^{2}+n\eta^{2},

\displaystyle\left|\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle=4\psi^{2}-4n\psi\eta+n^{2}\eta^{2}.

Using Integration by parts and (
4.30
) produces

\displaystyle{\left(\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},\mathfrak{h}\right)}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}={\left(\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right)}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}=-\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-n\left|\left|\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

In the end, the second variation can be written as

D^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=-\frac{1}{4n}{\left(\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2\left|\left|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+(n+2)\left|\left|\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\right)}.

The final part of the proof is most easily achieved by again decomposing
\mathfrak{h}
, this time by trace,
\mathfrak{h}=\theta\mathfrak{g}+\mathfrak{K}
, where

\theta=\frac{1}{n+1}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}=\frac{n\eta-2\psi}{n+1}

and

\mathfrak{K}=\mathfrak{h}-\theta\mathfrak{g}=-n\omega e^{-2\psi}\;d\tau^{2}+\omega g,\qquad\qquad\omega=\frac{\eta-2\psi}{n+1}.

It will be useful to keep the following identities in mind:

\displaystyle\left|\mathfrak{K}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=n\left(n+1\right)\omega^{2}

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{\tau i\tau}

\displaystyle=\left(\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{\tau i\tau}=-\left(n+1\right)\omega e^{-2\varphi}d\varphi_{i}

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{i\tau\tau}

\displaystyle=-ne^{2\varphi}d\omega_{i}

\displaystyle\left(\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{ijk}

\displaystyle=-d\omega_{i}g_{jk}

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=2\left(n+1\right)^{2}\omega^{2}\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}+n\left(n+1\right)\left|d\omega\right|^{2}

\displaystyle\left(\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{i}

\displaystyle=\left(n+1\right)\omega d\varphi_{i}-d\omega_{i}

\displaystyle\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|^{2}-2\left|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|^{2}

\displaystyle=\left(n^{2}+2n-2\right)\left|d\omega\right|^{2}+4\left(n+1\right)\omega\left<d\omega,d\varphi\right>

\displaystyle 2\left(d\theta,\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=-2n\left|\left|d\theta\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}-2n\left(n+1\right)\left|\left|\theta\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}.

We also note that, due to (
4.30
), we have

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle=\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+n\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}

\displaystyle=\left(n+1\right)\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\theta+n\left(n+1\right)\theta-\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\theta+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}

\displaystyle=n\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\theta+n\left(n+1\right)\theta+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}.

Using the above identities and the decomposition of
\mathfrak{h}
into full trace and trace–free parts, one may show that

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2\left|\left|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\theta\mathfrak{g}\right)+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2\left|\left|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\theta\mathfrak{g}\right)+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\theta\mathfrak{g}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}-2\left|\left|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\theta\mathfrak{g}\right)}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2\left|\left|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+2\left(\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\theta\mathfrak{g}\right),\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ \penalty\ -4\left(\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\left(\theta\mathfrak{g}\right),\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\left(n+1\right)\left|\left|d\theta\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}+\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}-2\left|\left|d\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2\left|\left|\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+2{\left(d\theta,\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\left(n-1\right)\left|\left|d\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+2\left(d\theta,\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\left(n^{2}+n-2\right)\left|\left|d\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+4\left(n+1\right)\int_{M}\omega\left<d\omega,d\varphi\right>dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle=-(n+1)\left|\left|d\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2n(n+1)\left|\left|\theta\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}+(n-1)(n+2)\left|\left|d\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+2n(n+1)\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

Note that the final line used the identity for
\left(d\theta,\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
which we stated earlier, as well as

\int_{M}\omega\left<d\omega,d\varphi\right>dV_{\mathfrak{g}}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\left<d\omega^{2},d\varphi\right>dV_{\mathfrak{g}}=\frac{1}{2}\int_{M}\omega^{2}\Delta_{\varphi}\varphi\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}=\frac{n}{2}\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

The final term above will be cancelled by a contribution from

-2\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}=-2(n+1)\left|\left|\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-2n(n+1)\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

Finally, using

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\left(n+1\right)^{2}\left|\left|d\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

\displaystyle\left|\left|\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\left(n+1\right)^{2}\left|\left|\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

the full second variation can be written as

D^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=-\frac{1}{4n}{\left(n(n+1)\left|\left|d\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+n(n+1)^{2}\left|\left|\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+(n-1)(n+2)\left|\left|d\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\right)}.

(4.31)

Using the expression for
\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|
from above, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\leq 2\left(n+1\right)^{2}\sup_{M}\left|d\varphi\right|\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+n\left(n+1\right)\left|\left|d\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

[
Lee
, Proposition F]
provides a spectral gap
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\geq\Lambda>0
, where
\Lambda=\frac{{\left(n-1\right)}^{2}}{4}
. This, in turn, gives the Poincaré inequality
\left|\left|\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\leq\frac{1}{\Lambda}\left|\left|d\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Combining this with the bound on the
L^{2}
-norm of
\nabla\mathfrak{K}
from above, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\leq\left(\frac{2\left(n+1\right)^{2}\sup_{M}\left|d\varphi\right|+\Lambda n\left(n+1\right)}{\Lambda}\right)\left|\left|d\omega\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

This, when combined with (
4.31
), yields

\displaystyle D^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{4n}\left(n\left(n+1\right)\left|\left|\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\frac{\left(n-1\right)\left(n+2\right)\Lambda}{2\left(n+1\right)^{2}\sup_{M}\left|d\varphi\right|+\Lambda n\left(n+1\right)}\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq-\frac{1}{4n}\left(n\left(n+1\right)\left|\left|\theta\right|\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\frac{\left(n-1\right)\left(n+2\right)\Lambda}{4\left(n+1\right)^{2}\sup_{M}\left|d\varphi\right|+2\Lambda n\left(n+1\right)}\left(\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\widetilde{\Lambda}\left|\left|\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\leq-C_{g,\varphi}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

where the penultimate line follows from applying a spectral gap argument to the trace–free
\mathfrak{K}
. Specifically, using
[
Lee
, Proposition E]
to guarantee that
\widetilde{\Lambda}\left|\left|\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\leq\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
, for
\widetilde{\Lambda}=\frac{{\left(n-1\right)}^{2}}{4}+2
.
∎

5
A Local Positive Mass Theorem

In this section, we prove a local positive mass theorem which, in essence, shows that positivity of the weighted mass

m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}=-\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}\left\langle\delta_{\widehat{g}}h+d\tr_{\widehat{g}}h,\widehat{\nu}\right\rangle_{\widehat{g}}+\tr_{\widehat{g}}h\;\widehat{\nu}{\left(\varphi\right)}-h{\left(d\varphi,\widehat{\nu}\right)}\;d\widehat{A}_{\varphi}

for certain
h\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}(M)\cap H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}(M)
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
, is tantamount to the static pair
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
being a local maximum of the entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}
. This, in turn, will be proven to be equivalent to dynamical stability under the Ricci-harmonic flow in Section
6
.

A subtle difference between the current section and the next, is that we require all metrics in this section to have a certain pointwise, in addition to being
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
-close to a static metric
\widehat{g}
. To be specific, for the rest of Section
5
and unless stated otherwise, we assume
g\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\cap\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
, where
\alpha\in[0,1)
and
\beta\in{\left(\frac{n}{2},n\right)}
. In Section
6
, we drop the requirement that
g\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Consider a static background pair
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
and fix a symmetric
2
-tensor
h\in H^{1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
. For any other pair
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
we have

m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(h,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}=m_{g}{\left(h,\varphi\right)},

provided
\widehat{\varphi}-\varphi\in H^{1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M\right)}
,
\left|\widehat{g}-g\right|+\left|\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n{\left(n-1\right)}\right|=\mathcal{O}{\left(e^{-\beta r}\right)}
for some
\beta>\frac{n}{2}
. In particular, it is meaningful to discuss the mass relative to non–static metric pairs
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
.

Proof.

The result follows from analysing the error terms that result from changing from quantities defined with respect to
\widehat{g}
and/or
\widehat{\varphi}
to those defined with respect to
g
and/or
\varphi
. For instance,

\tr_{g}h=\tr_{\widehat{g}}h+\tr_{g}h-\tr_{\widehat{g}}h=\tr_{\widehat{g}}h+\left(g^{-1}-\widehat{g}^{-1}\right)\ast h.

Using our decay assumption on
\widehat{g}-g
, and that
h\in H^{1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
, implies the boundary integrals involving the error term
\left(g^{-1}-\widehat{g}^{-1}\right)\ast h
vanish as
R\rightarrow\infty
. The rest of the proof follows along similar lines, after using the formula

\nabla_{g}T=\nabla_{\widehat{g}}T+\widehat{g}^{-1}\ast\nabla_{g}h\ast T

(5.2)

to deal with the divergence term.
∎

5.1
A Conformal Positive Mass Result

An integral part of the local positive mass theorem, Theorem
5.14
, is the following
conformal
positive mass proposition.

Proposition 5.3
.

Let
g\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
be a metric of constant scalar curvature,
\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n(n-1)
, and let
\varphi
be an
exact potential
– that is, an approximate potential satisfying the defining equation exactly;

\Delta_{g}\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|_{g}^{2}=n.

(5.4)

Furthermore, let
\widetilde{g}\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
be a metric conformal to
g
such that
\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}\geq-n(n-1)
. Then,

m_{g}{\left(\widetilde{g}-g,\varphi\right)}\geq 0,

and equality holds if and only if
\widetilde{g}=g
.

Remark 5.5
.

Note that the assumptions on
g
and
\varphi
imply that the auxiliary metric
e^{-2\varphi}\;d\tau^{2}+g
has constant scalar curvature
-n(n+1)
. Note also that for a metric
g\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
, we can always find an exact potential due to perturbation theory. That is, one linearises (
5.4
) at
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
and applies the implicit function theorem.

Proof of Proposition
5.3
.

We parametrise the conformal factor such that
\widetilde{g}=\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}}g
, where
\phi=1+\mathcal{O}(e^{-\beta r})
. The scalar curvatures of the two metrics are related by

\frac{4(n-1)}{n-2}\Delta_{g}\phi=\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}\phi^{\frac{n+2}{n-2}}-\mathrm{scal}_{g}\phi.

(5.6)

Since
\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}\geq\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n(n-1)
, a maximum principle argument shows that
\phi\geq 1
(see the proof of
[
DKM
, Proposition 4.5]
). This is crucial later in the proof.

Inserting
h=\widetilde{g}-g=(\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}}-1)g
in the definition of the weighted ADM mass, and substituting
V:=e^{-\varphi}
, yields

m_{g}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}=(n-1)\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}\left((\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}}-1)\widehat{\nu}(V)-V\widehat{\nu}(\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}})\right)dA_{g}.

Now consider the equation

\Delta_{g}W=-n\phi^{p}W

(5.7)

where
p
is a large exponent which will be chosen later. We can find a solution by considering the equivalent equation

(\Delta_{g}+n\phi^{p})(W-V)=n(1-\phi^{p})V.

(5.8)

The operator on the left hand side is strictly positive and therefore has trivial
L^{2}
-kernel. Because
\phi\to 1
at infinity,
\Delta_{g}+n\phi^{p}:C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}\to C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
has the same indicial radius as
\Delta_{g}+n
, and is therefore an isomorphism for all
\beta\in(-1,n)
by Proposition
2.27
. Since the right hand side of (
5.8
) is contained in
C^{0,\alpha}_{\beta-1}{\left(M\right)}
, we can solve this equation and get
W-V\in C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta-1}{\left(M\right)}
. In particular,
W
is positive in a neighbourhood of infinity because
V
is. Moreover, a maximum principle argument applied to the equation shows that
W\geq 0
everywhere.

The decay is strong enough, that the mass remains unchanged under the substitution of
V
by
W
;

m_{g}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}=(n-1)\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}\left((\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}}-1)\widehat{\nu}(W)-W\widehat{\nu}(\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}})\right)dA_{g},

By the decay assumptions of
\phi
at infinity, we have

\displaystyle-(n-1)\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}W\widehat{\nu}(\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}})\;dA_{g}

\displaystyle=-4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}W\phi^{\frac{6-n}{n-2}}\widehat{\nu}(\phi)\;dA_{g}

\displaystyle=-4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}W\widehat{\nu}(\phi)\;dA_{g}.

By Green’s formula, this can be further modified to

\displaystyle-4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}W\widehat{\nu}(\phi)\;dA_{g}

\displaystyle=-4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}(\phi-1)\widehat{\nu}(W)\;dA_{g}

\displaystyle+4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{B_{R}}(W\Delta_{g}\phi-(\phi-1)\Delta_{g}W)\;dV_{g}.

Now we want to substitute this into the above formula for
m_{g}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}
. For this purpose, we note that Taylor expansion implies

(n-1)(\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}}-1)-4\frac{n-1}{n-2}(\phi-1)=\mathcal{O}{\left((\phi-1)^{2}\right)}=\mathcal{O}{\left(e^{-2\beta r}\right)}.

Therefore

(n-1)\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}(\phi^{\frac{4}{n-2}}-1)\widehat{\nu}(W)\;dA_{g}=4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}(\phi-1)\widehat{\nu}(W)\;dA_{g}.

Putting everything together, and using (
5.6
) and (
5.7
), we get

\displaystyle m_{g}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}

\displaystyle=4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\int_{M}(W\Delta_{g}\phi-(\phi-1)\Delta_{g}W)dV_{g}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}W\left(\phi^{\frac{n+2}{n-2}}\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}-\phi\mathrm{scal}_{g}+4n\frac{n-1}{n-2}(\phi-1)\phi^{p}\right)dV_{g}

\displaystyle=\int_{M}W\left(\phi^{\frac{n+2}{n-2}}(\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}+n(n-1))+n(n-1)F(\phi)\right)dV_{g},

where we used that
\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n(n-1)
, and define
F
by

F(x)=x-x^{\frac{n+2}{n-2}}+\frac{4}{n-2}(x-1)x^{p}.

One computes
F(1)=0
and
F^{\prime}(0)=1
for any choice of
p>1
. Furthermore, we have

\displaystyle F^{\prime\prime}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{4}{n-2}\left[(p+1)px^{p-1}-p(p-1)x^{p-2}-\frac{n+2}{n-2}x^{\frac{6-n}{n-2}}\right]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{4}{n-2}\left[2px^{p-1}-\frac{n+2}{n-2}x^{\frac{6-n}{n-2}}\right]

for
x\geq 1
. Therefore, if
p
is chosen so large that

p>\max\left\{\frac{4}{n-2},\frac{1}{2}\frac{n+2}{n-2}\right\},

then
F^{\prime\prime}(x)>0
for all
x\geq 1
, so that
F(x)>0
for all
x\geq 1
by the above. Then, since
\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}\geq-n(n-1)
,
\phi\geq 1
and
W\geq 0
, we get that

m_{g}{\left(h,\varphi\right)}=\int_{M}W\left(\phi^{\frac{n+2}{n-2}}(\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}+n(n-1))+F(\phi)\right)dV_{g}\geq 0,

and equality can only hold if
\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}=-n(n-1)
and
\phi=1
. This finishes the proof of the proposition.
∎

5.2
Proof of Theorem
1.8

We now prove some technical estimates we will need when proving our local positive mass theorem (Theorem
1.8
/
5.14
).

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple. Then, for
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
, there exists a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
and a constant
C>0
, such that for any
\mathfrak{g}=(g,\varphi)\in\mathcal{U}
, the following estimates hold:

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}u_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}},

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}u_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\>\;

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}f_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;\;

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\left|\left|\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}u_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\,

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

where
f_{\mathfrak{g}}=f_{g}
is the unique minimiser of the entropy given in Theorem
3.25
,
u_{\mathfrak{g}}:=f_{\mathfrak{g}}-\varphi
, and
\mathfrak{h}={\left(h,\psi\right)}\in H^{\ell}_{\mathfrak{g}}
.

Proof.

The static metric will stay mostly in the background for this proof. However, we use the fact that any pair
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
has
u_{g,\varphi}\in H^{\ell}_{\varphi}\subset C^{2,\alpha}
(see Propositions
2.14
and
3.27
), and that the auxiliary Ricci curvature of
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
has finite
C^{0,\alpha}
-norm. With that out of the way, we may suppress the
g,\varphi
subscripts to simplify notation. Recall that
u
satisfies (
3.20
):

\Delta u+\frac{1}{2}\left|du\right|^{2}+\left\langle d\varphi,du\right\rangle+nu=-{\left(\Delta\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}-n\right)}+\frac{1}{2}{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n\left(n-1\right)\right)},

or, in terms of the auxiliary metric
\mathfrak{g}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
, as
