\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}u+nu+\frac{1}{2}\left|du\right|^{2}=\frac{1}{2}{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n(n+1)\right)}.

(5.10)

Taking the variation of this equation, and denoting the variation of
u
by
u^{\prime}
, yields

\begin{split}{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}u^{\prime}={}&\frac{1}{2}\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}-\left(\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}\right)u+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{h}\left(\nabla u,\nabla u\right)-\left\langle du^{\prime},du\right\rangle\\
={}&\frac{1}{2}\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}-\left<\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h}\right>\right)-\left<\mathfrak{h},\nabla^{2}u\right>\\
&+\left<\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\frac{1}{2}d\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},du\right>+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{h}\left(\nabla u,\nabla u\right)-\left\langle du^{\prime},du\right\rangle\\
={}&\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\nabla^{2}u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u.\end{split}

(5.11)

Since
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n:C^{2,\alpha}\rightarrow C^{0,\alpha}
is an isomorphism by Proposition
2.27
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq{}

\displaystyle C\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\nabla^{2}u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle C^{\prime}{\left(\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}^{2}+1\right)}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}+C\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}}.

Since the
C^{2,\alpha}
-norm of
u
is controlled by the proximity to the background (which has
u\equiv 0
), we may assume that we are working in a neighbourhood so small that
\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq\frac{1}{2C}
. Moving the final term in the above equation to the left hand side, we obtain the desired:

\frac{1}{2}\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq C^{\prime}{\left({\left(\frac{1}{2C^{\prime\prime}}\right)}^{2}+1\right)}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}.

As for the corresponding estimate involving
H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-norms, we can integrate (
5.11
) against
u^{\prime}
to arrive at

\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}\leq\Big(u^{\prime},\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\nabla^{2}u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u\Big)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

(5.12)

The next step is a little delicate, as we will have to get rid of the second order derivative of
\mathfrak{h}
. In this case, we may recall the precise form of those terms, which will allow us to integrate by parts:

\displaystyle\Big(u^{\prime},\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}\Big)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(u^{\prime},\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+d\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right)}\Big)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Big(du^{\prime},\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+d\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\Big)_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}-\frac{1}{2}\lim_{R\to\infty}\int_{\partial B_{R}}u^{\prime}\left\langle\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+d\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},\nu\right\rangle\;dA_{\mathfrak{g}}.

By the first proven inequality of this lemma, and the bounded
H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-norm of
\mathfrak{h}
, the boundary integral vanishes in the limit. Returning to (
5.12
), we proceed by applying Cauchy–Schwarz and factoring out bounded Hölder norms of
u
and
\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}
:

\displaystyle\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\Big(\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\nabla^{2}u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq\widetilde{C}\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\Big(1+\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\Big)\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2}

After possibly shrinking the size of the neighbourhood we are working in, we can ensure
\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, so that we may absorb the final term on the right into the left hand side. Dividing by
\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
, all that remains is

\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\leq 3\widetilde{C}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

which proves the second inequality.

For the third inequality, we rearrange (
5.11
) slightly, by absorbing
{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}\psi
into the right hand side, to yield:

\displaystyle{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}v

\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\nabla^{2}u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u+n\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u.

Then, using that
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n:H^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\rightarrow L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}
is an isomorphism by Proposition
2.27
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|v\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{C}\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+\nabla^{2}u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u+n\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\leq\widehat{C}^{\prime}{\left(1+\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\right)}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\widehat{C}\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{3\widehat{C}^{\prime}+\widehat{C}}{2}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}},

where the final inequality follows from the previous estimate, and the assumptions made during its proof.

For the final estimate of the lemma we take the variation of (
5.11
) to obtain

\displaystyle\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)u^{\prime\prime}={}

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}-{\left(\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}\right)}u-{\left(\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}}\right)}u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+2\mathfrak{h}{\left(\nabla u^{\prime},\nabla u\right)}-\frac{1}{2}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\right)}{\left(\nabla u,\nabla u\right)}-\left|du^{\prime}\right|^{2}-\left\langle du^{\prime\prime},du\right\rangle

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\mathfrak{h}+{\left(\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+\nabla u^{\prime}\right)}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u^{\prime}

\displaystyle+{\left(R_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla^{2}u^{\prime}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u\ast\mathfrak{h}\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime\prime}\ast\nabla u.

where the schematic expression in the last two lines can be derived from
[
K
, Lemma A.3]
. It is written in a deliberate way, according to the method of estimation in the following.

By integrating against
u^{\prime\prime}
and using Hölder’s inequality, one sees that

\displaystyle\left|\left|u^{\prime\prime}\right|\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\leq{}

\displaystyle\overline{C}\big|\big|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\mathfrak{h}+{\left(\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+\nabla u^{\prime}\right)}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u^{\prime}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+{\left(R_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla^{2}u^{\prime}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u\ast\mathfrak{h}\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}\big|\big|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\left|\left|u^{\prime\prime}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\leq{}

\displaystyle\overline{C}^{\prime}\bigg(\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla u+\nabla u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\left|\left|\nabla u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}}\left|\left|u^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\left|\left|R_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla^{2}u^{\prime}+\nabla u^{\prime}\ast\nabla u+\nabla u\ast\nabla u\ast\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{0,\alpha}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}\bigg)\left|\left|u^{\prime\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

Combining this with the previous estimates and the uniform
C^{2,\alpha}
-bound on
u
, and then dividing through by
\left|\left|u^{\prime\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}
, completes the proof.
∎

Proposition 5.13
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple and
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
. Then there exists a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
and a constant
C>0
, such that, for any
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{U}
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left.\frac{d^{3}}{ds^{3}}\right|_{s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}\right)}\right|\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}}^{2},

where
\mathfrak{g}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
and
\mathfrak{h}=(h,\psi)
in the usual sense (of Section
4.2
).

Proof.

As we are interested in the auxiliary norms, we extend the first variation (
4.5
), to an integral over
\mathfrak{M}=\mathbb{S}^{1}\times M
:

\displaystyle-4n\pi D_{\mathfrak{g}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}.

where
u_{\mathfrak{g}}=f_{\mathfrak{g}}-\varphi
as in Lemma
5.9
. Writing
k_{\mathfrak{g}}:=\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}
, we find that

\displaystyle-4n\pi\left.\frac{d^{3}}{ds^{3}}\right|_{s=0}

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}+s\mathfrak{h}\right)}

\displaystyle={}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle k_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle k_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime},\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}-2{\left(\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\right)}-2u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle k_{\mathfrak{g}},{\left(\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\right)}-{\left(\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}-2u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\right)}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\times\mathfrak{h}\right)}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}

\displaystyle+\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle k_{\mathfrak{g}},{\left(\frac{1}{2}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}-u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\right)}^{2}\mathfrak{h}-\frac{1}{2}\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}-u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime}\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}},

where we use the convention:

h\times k\times l=h\times{\left(k\times l\right)}+{\left(h\times k\right)}\times l+{\left(h\times l\right)}\times k.

Using Hamilton star notation and referencing
[
K
, Appendix A]
, we may write

\displaystyle k_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime}={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\mathfrak{h}+R_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}\ast\mathfrak{h}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}^{2}u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}

\displaystyle k_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+R_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h},

where
R_{\mathfrak{g}}
is a catch-all for curvature terms and constants. The terms
k_{\mathfrak{g}}
,
R_{\mathfrak{g}}
,
u_{\mathfrak{g}}
and
\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}
are uniformly bounded due to proximity to a static background. By counting the number and derivatives of
\mathfrak{h}
and
u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime}
in each term, it is a simple task to construct the claimed inequality via Lemma
5.9
, Hölder’s inequality, and the definition of the neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}
. At least for every term save one. The only term that requires special attention is

\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}.

Using Lemma
5.9
, we deduce that
u^{\prime\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}}
has bounded
H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-norm, as long as
\mathfrak{h}
has the same. As such, no boundary term arises from integration by parts:

\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime},\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}=\int_{\mathfrak{M}}\left\langle du_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime},\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right\rangle_{\mathfrak{g}}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}+\mathfrak{h}{\left(\nabla u_{\mathfrak{g}}^{\prime\prime},\nabla u_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\;dV_{\mathfrak{g}}.

The proposition now follows by a further use of Hölder’s inequality and Lemma
5.9
.
∎

We can now prove Theorem
1.8
, which we restate here in an expanded version for the reader’s convenience.

Theorem 5.14
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple,
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
and
\frac{n}{2}<\beta<n
. Then the following are equivalent:

1.

{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
is a local minimiser of the functional
(g,\varphi)\mapsto m_{\widehat{g}}(g-\widehat{g},\varphi)
on

\mathcal{D}:=\big\{{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\;:\;\mathrm{scal}_{g}\geq-n{\left(n-1\right)}\big\}.

2.

{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
is a local minimiser of the functional
(g,\varphi)\mapsto m_{\widehat{g}}(g-\widehat{g},\varphi)
on

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}:=\big\{(g,\varphi)\;:\;\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n{\left(n-1\right)},\;\Delta_{g}\varphi+\left|d\varphi\right|^{2}_{g}=n\big\}.

3.

{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
is a local maximiser of
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
on
\mathcal{C}
.

4.

{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
is a local maximiser of
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
.

The statements relate to metric pairs
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\cap\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
.

Proof.

Note that
(1.)
implies
(2.)
, simply as
\mathcal{C}
is contained in
\mathcal{D}
.
To show that
(2.)
implies
(1.)
, consider the pair
{\left(g_{\mathcal{D}},\varphi_{\mathcal{D}}\right)}\in\mathcal{D}
. By the resolution of the Yamabe problem (see Theorem
A.13
), we may find a CSC metric
g_{\mathcal{C}}\in\left[g_{\mathcal{D}}\right]
, the conformal class of
g_{\mathcal{D}}
in
\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\cap\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
. Relative to this metric, we may even find an exact potential
\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}
(see Remark
5.5
) such that
{\left(g_{\mathcal{C}},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}\in\mathcal{C}
. Then, since the mass is linear in its first argument, we can compute as follows:

\displaystyle m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g_{\mathcal{D}}-\widehat{g},\varphi_{\mathcal{D}}\right)}

\displaystyle=m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g_{\mathcal{D}}-\widehat{g},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}

\displaystyle=m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g_{\mathcal{D}}-g_{\mathcal{C}},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}+m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g_{\mathcal{C}}-\widehat{g},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq m_{g_{\mathcal{C}}}{\left(g_{\mathcal{D}}-g_{\mathcal{C}},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}

\displaystyle\geq 0,

where the first equality follows from Lemma
5.1
, the first inequality from the assumption that
(2.)
holds, while the final inequality is due to Proposition
5.3
. In other words,
(1.)
holds.

For an arbitrary
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{C}
, Proposition
3.14
tells us
f_{g}=\varphi
. Plugging this and
\mathrm{scal}_{g}=-n{\left(n-1\right)}
into the definition of the entropy tells us
\mathcal{B}_{R}{\left(g,f_{g},\varphi\right)}=0
and
\mathcal{W}_{R}{\left(g,f_{g},\varphi\right)}=0
. Thus

\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}=-m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g-\widehat{g},\varphi\right)}.

Hence, if
(2.)
holds, we have
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\leq 0
, while if
(3.)
holds we have
m_{\widehat{g}}{\left(g-\widehat{g},\varphi\right)}\geq 0
. That is,
(2.)
and
(3.)
are equivalent.

We next note that
(4.)
implies
(3.)
, by again using that
\mathcal{C}
is a subset of
\mathcal{D}
.

Now assume
(3.)
holds and consider an arbitrary pair
\mathfrak{g}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
with
g\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\cap\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
and
\varphi\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
. As before, we conclude from Theorem
A.13
that
g
is conformally related to a metric
{g}_{\mathcal{C}}
with exact potential
\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}
, such that
{\left(g_{\mathcal{C}},\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}\in\mathcal{C}
. We write
g={\left(1+\eta\right)}{g}_{\mathcal{C}}
with
\eta\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M\right)}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
. By setting
\mathfrak{h}:={\left(e^{-2\varphi}-e^{-2\varphi_{\mathcal{C}}}\right)}d\tau^{2}+\eta g_{\mathcal{C}}
, we may write
\mathfrak{g}={\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{C}}+\mathfrak{h}
.

To understand the relationship between the entropy of
\mathfrak{g}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{C}}
, we Taylor expand around the latter metric with respect to the affine curve connecting them:

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{C}}+s\mathfrak{h}\right)}+\frac{1}{2}\left.\frac{d^{2}}{ds^{2}}\right|_{s=0}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{C}}+s\mathfrak{h}\right)}

\displaystyle+\frac{1}{2}\int^{1}_{0}\left(1-s\right)^{2}\frac{d^{3}}{ds^{3}}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left({\mathfrak{g}}_{\mathcal{C}}+s\mathfrak{h}\right)}\;ds.

Using Propositions
4.26
and
5.13
, we find that the first derivative vanishes, the second has a negative upper bound, and the third has a uniform bound for all
s\in[0,1]
. In particular,

\displaystyle\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{2}{\left(C_{1}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}-C_{2}\right)}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|^{2}_{H^{1}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{C}}}},

where
C_{1},C_{2}
are the constants from the propositions referenced above. By restricting our attention to a sufficiently small
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-neighbourhood of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
, we may assume

\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}<\frac{C_{2}}{C_{1}},

which means
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\mathcal{C}}\right)}
, with equality if and only if
\mathfrak{h}=0
. This concludes the proof.
∎

6
The Stability and Instability Results

This section is devoted to proving Theorem
1.9
and Theorem
1.10
. First, though, we need to prove a weighted Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality (Theorem
1.12
) for pairs
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
which are sufficiently close to a static pair
\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)
. We remind the reader that we shall now drop the pointwise decay condition,
g\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
, of the preceding section.

6.1
The Łojasiewicz–Simon Inequality

As in, say,
[
KY
]
, the proof of the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality will follow the framework introduced in
[
CM
]
. This in turn relies on proving various continuity and Fredholm properties of the entropy and its derivatives, when they act as maps between suitable Sobolev spaces. We emphasise here that the norms involved in such estimates will implicitly be taken with respect to a fixed static pair
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
.

Proposition 6.1
.

For
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
and any
\left(g_{1},\varphi_{1}\right)
,
\left(g_{2},\varphi_{2}\right)
, which are
\varepsilon
-close to
\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)
in the
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
-sense for
0<\varepsilon\ll 1
, we have

\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g_{2},\varphi_{2}\right)}-\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g_{1},\varphi_{1}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}\leq C{\left(\varepsilon\right)}\left|\left|{\left(g_{2},\varphi_{2}\right)}-{\left(g_{1},\varphi_{1}\right)}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}.

(6.2)

Furthermore, for any
h\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}
and
\psi\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M\right)}
, we have the following estimate for the difference of linearisations of the gradient of the entropy:

\left|\left|D_{{\left(g_{2},\varphi_{2}\right)}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(h,\psi\right)}-D_{{\left(g_{1},\varphi_{1}\right)}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(h,\psi\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}\leq C{\left(\varepsilon\right)}\left|\left|{\left(g_{2},\varphi_{2}\right)}-{\left(g_{1},\varphi_{1}\right)}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}.

(6.3)

Proof.

Using the auxiliary expression for the
L^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}
-gradient of the entropy, provided by Corollary
4.2
, we may complete the proof as a (almost) faithful rendition of the proof of
[
KY
, Proposition 5.9]
. With the usual notation,
\mathfrak{g}=(g,\varphi)
and
u_{\mathfrak{g}}=f_{\mathfrak{g}}-\varphi
, we have the gradient

\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}=-\frac{1}{2n}{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}^{2}u_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

The proof of (
6.2
) boils down to showing

\displaystyle\left|\left|\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

(6.4)

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

(6.5)

\displaystyle\left|\left|e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}}-e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

(6.6)

For the first two, (
6.4
)-(
6.5
), it is advantageous to use the schematic expressions

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{2}\ast{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}

(6.7)

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}\ast d{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)},

(6.8)

where the suppressed contractions are all with respect to
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
. The above equations can be found on page 114 of
[
CLN
]
. It should be noted that the proximity of
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
to the background
\mathfrak{g}
, is such as to make the difference between volume forms and covariant derivatives negligible. To put it simply, we are interested in the order of
\nabla
, not the subscript. In light of (
6.7
), we see that (
6.4
) holds as
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
and
\mathfrak{g}_{2}
are sufficiently close to
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
to ensure

\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{2}\ast{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C(\varepsilon)\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

For the difference of Hessians, (
6.5
), we need to show that
\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}
can somehow be estimated by
\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}
. To this end we write
\mathfrak{g}_{s}=\mathfrak{g}_{1}+(s-1){\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}
, which means

\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}=\int_{1}^{2}\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{2}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{s}}\;ds,

where
u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{s}}=\left.\frac{d}{dt}\right|_{t=0}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{s+t}}
. Combined with the third inequality of Lemma
5.9
, we have the desired estimate

\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq\sup_{s\in[1,2]}\left|\left|u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{s}}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

One may even use the argument to conclude that

\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

from which (
6.6
) follows. The inequality will be needed again in the second part of this proof. From Proposition
3.27
and the Sobolev embedding theorem, we have
\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C\varepsilon
, with which we may complete the proof of (
6.2
):

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+C\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right|\right|_{C^{1}}\left|\left|\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon)\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

where that the first inequality uses (
6.8
).

As for the proof of (
6.3
), we may linearise the gradient using standard variational formulae (see Appendix
A
). This yields

\displaystyle D_{\mathfrak{g}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=-\frac{e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}}{2n}\biggl(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{L}\mathfrak{h}+\frac{1}{2}\mathfrak{h}\times\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}-\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}{\left(\beta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}+\mathfrak{h}\cdot du_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}+n\mathfrak{h}\;\;

\displaystyle+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}}-u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}

\displaystyle\biggr),

where
\beta_{\mathfrak{g}}=\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+\frac{1}{2}d\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}
is the Bianchi operator. In light of past estimates, the only term we need to prove new estimates for is
\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}
. To this end, first note that by applying (
6.8
) we have

\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}=\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right)}\ast d{\left(u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}-u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}.

Therefore, we can estimate as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+\left|\left|u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right|\right|_{C^{1}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\right)

\displaystyle\leq C\left(\left|\left|\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}-u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{2}-\mathfrak{g}_{1}\right|\right|_{H^{1}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\right),

where the final line is due to the first estimate of Proposition
5.9
. Next, by taking the difference of the linearised Euler–Lagrange equations (
5.11
) for
u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{\prime}
and
u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}^{\prime}
, one finds that

\displaystyle{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}+n\right)}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{\prime}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}^{\prime}\right)}={}

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\mathfrak{K}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\mathfrak{K}\ast{\big[\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\mathfrak{K}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\mathfrak{h}+\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}+u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}\big]}+\mathfrak{K}\ast\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\mathfrak{K}\ast{\big[\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}+u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}+\mathfrak{h}\ast{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}\big]}

\displaystyle+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{2}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}\ast\mathfrak{h}+\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}\ast{\big[\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\mathfrak{h}+\mathfrak{h}\ast\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}{\left(u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}+u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}\big]}

where
\mathfrak{K}:=\mathfrak{g}_{1}-\mathfrak{g}_{2}
and we allow
\ast
to denote contractions in both metrics. Thus, using that
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}+n:H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\rightarrow L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
is an isomorphism by a slight generalisation of Proposition
2.27
, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{\prime}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}+n\right)}{\left(u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}-u^{\prime}_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\varepsilon){\left(\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}+\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}^{\prime}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\right)}\left|\left|\mathfrak{K}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+C(\varepsilon)\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

where we, for the sake of the reader, have already estimated the factors
\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}
and
\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}
using
\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{i}}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\leq C(\varepsilon)
for
i=1,2
. In the first half of the proof, it was shown that

\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{1}-\mathfrak{g}_{2}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

which, combined with the first inequality of Lemma
5.9
, proves that

\left|\left|u_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}^{\prime}-u_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}^{\prime}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C(\varepsilon)\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{1}-\mathfrak{g}_{2}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

With this in hand, we may conclude that

\left|\left|D_{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}-D_{\mathfrak{g}_{2}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C{\left(\varepsilon\right)}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{1}-\mathfrak{g}_{2}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

which, due to the Sobolev embedding theorem, is simply an auxiliary version of (
6.3
).
∎

We will need the following slice theorem before we are ready to prove the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple, let
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
, and define the space of transverse auxiliary metrics

\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}:=\left\{{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}:e^{-2\varphi}d\tau^{2}+g=\mathfrak{g}\in\ker\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\right\}.

Then there is a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
of
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
, such that any pair
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{U}
can be written uniquely as
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}={\left(\eta^{\ast}\widetilde{g},\eta^{\ast}\widetilde{\varphi}\right)}
for some
{\left(\widetilde{g},\widetilde{\varphi}\right)}\in\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}
, and a diffeomorphism
\eta\in\mathrm{Diff}^{\ell+1}{\left(M\right)}
that is
H^{\ell+1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
-close to the identity.

Proof.

As
{\left(\mathfrak{M},\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}
is AH, one may show that the operator
\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
has indicial radius
R=\frac{n}{2}
(when
\dim\mathfrak{M}=n+1
). One way to see this is to decompose it as

2\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\delta^{*}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}X={\left(\nabla^{*}\nabla\right)}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}X-\mathrm{Ric}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot X+\nabla_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}X,

and use that
-\mathrm{Ric}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\cdot X\sim nX
(an identity in the case of the PE metric
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
, but asymptotically true for a general AH metric). One may then show that
R
is determined solely as the indicial radius of
{\left(\nabla^{*}\nabla\right)}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}+n
, which is indeed
\frac{n}{2}
. This, and ellipticity, implies that
\mathrm{Im}\delta^{*}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}|_{H^{\ell+1}_{\beta}{\left(T\mathfrak{M}\right)}}
is closed for
\beta
in the Fredholm range of
\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}^{*}
, i.e.
\left|\beta\right|<\frac{n}{2}
. Specifically, for
\beta=0
, we have the decomposition

H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(S^{2}\mathfrak{M}\right)}=\mathrm{ker}\left.\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(S^{2}\mathfrak{M}\right)}}\oplus\mathrm{Im}\left.\delta^{\ast}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\right|_{H^{\ell+1}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(T\mathfrak{M}\right)}}.

By restricting the decomposition to the subspace
T_{{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}}{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\right)}
, we obtain

\displaystyle T_{{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}}{\left(\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\right)}

\displaystyle=\left\{{\left(h,\psi\right)}\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(S^{2}M\right)}\times H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M\right)}:\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}(h,\psi)=0\right\}\oplus\left\{{\left(\delta^{\ast}_{\widehat{g}}X,\frac{1}{2}X{\left(\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\right)}:X\in H^{\ell+1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(TM\right)}\right\},

where
\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}(h,\psi)=\delta_{\widehat{g}}h+h\cdot d\widehat{\varphi}+2\psi\;d\widehat{\varphi}
by (
2.19
). Note that the first factor on the right hand side above is exactly
T_{{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}}\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}
. Now define the map

\begin{cases}\Phi:\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}\times\mathrm{Diff}^{\ell+1}{\left(M\right)}&\longrightarrow\;\;\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\\
\hfill{\left(g,\varphi,\eta\right)}&\longmapsto\;\;{\left(\eta^{\ast}g,\eta^{\ast}\varphi\right)}.\end{cases}

The differential of
\Phi
at
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi},\mathrm{Id}_{M}\right)}
is

\begin{cases}D_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi},\mathrm{Id}_{M}}\Phi:T_{{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}}\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}\times H^{\ell+1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(TM\right)}&\longrightarrow\;\;T_{{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}}\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}\oplus\mathrm{Im}\>\delta^{\ast}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}|_{H^{\ell+1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(TM\right)}}\\
\hfill{\left(h,\psi,X\right)}&\longmapsto\;\;{\left(h+\delta^{\ast}_{\widehat{g}}X,\psi+\frac{1}{2}X{\left(\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\right)},\end{cases}

which is clearly bijective. The inverse function theorem then informs us that there exists a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
, such that every
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\in\mathcal{U}
can be written uniquely as
\Phi{\left(\widetilde{g},\widetilde{\varphi},\eta\right)}={\left(g,\varphi\right)}
, for some
{\left(\widetilde{g},\widetilde{\varphi}\right)}\in\mathcal{U}\cap\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}
and
\eta\in\mathrm{Diff}^{\ell+1}{\left(M\right)}
that is
H^{\ell+1}_{\widehat{\varphi}}
-close to
\mathrm{Id}_{M}
.
∎

For the next proof, we shall need the following generalisation of the space in Lemma
6.9
. For
1\leq k\leq\ell
, set

\mathcal{S}^{k}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}=\mathcal{S}^{k}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}:={\left(\mathcal{R}^{k}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{k}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\right)}\cap\ker\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}.

This definition is naturally extended to
k=0
, by setting

\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}=\mathcal{S}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}^{0}:={\left(\mathcal{R}^{k}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{k}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\right)}\cap{\left(\mathrm{Im}\;\delta^{*}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}|_{C_{c}^{\infty}(TM)}\right)}^{\perp}.

The precise form of
\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}}
is needed because of regularity considerations. We refer the reader to
[
KY
, Lemma 5.7]
and the associated discussion for more details. We can now prove our Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality, which we restate here in an expanded form.

Theorem 6.10
.

Given a static pair
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
, there is a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\times\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\varphi}}{\left(M,\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
of
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
, in which the entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}=\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}
satisfies the following: there exists a positive constant
C>0
and an exponent
\theta\in\left(0,1\right]
, such that

\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}\right|^{2-\theta}\leq C\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(g,\varphi\right)}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\varphi}}}.

(6.11)

This weighted inequality is equivalent to an unweighted inequality with respect to the auxiliary metric
\\
wh{\mathfrak{g}}=e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}d\tau^{2}+\widehat{g}
:

\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{2-\theta}\leq C\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}\right|\right|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

(6.12)

Proof.

The result will follow if we can ensure the assumptions and Condition
(1.)
-
(4.)
of
[
KY
, Theorem 5.1]
are met. The setup is slightly different from that of
[
KY
, Theorem 5.1]
, as we shall use
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
in place of
0
, and

\mathcal{R}^{k}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}(\mathfrak{M},\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}):=\left\{\mathfrak{g}\;:\;\mathfrak{g}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\in H^{k}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}(S^{2}\mathfrak{M})\right\}

in place of
H^{k}
. However, this causes no issues. By Lemma
6.9
and the diffeomorphism invariance of the entropy, it is enough to prove (
6.11
) on a neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}\subset\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(\mathfrak{M},\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\cap\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}=\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
. Suppose
\mathcal{U}
is chosen small enough that
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
is well-defined and analytic, and the estimates of Proposition
6.1
and Lemma
5.9
hold.

Condition 1:

The
L^{2}
-gradient of
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
,
\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}:\mathcal{U}\to H^{\ell-2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
, satisfies
\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}})=0
and
\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\mathfrak{g}_{1})-\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\mathfrak{g}_{2})\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}_{1}-\mathfrak{g}_{2}\right|\right|_{H^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}
, for
\mathfrak{g}_{1},\mathfrak{g}_{2}\in\mathcal{U}
.

The
L^{2}
-gradient of
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
at
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathcal{U}
is given by

\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\mathfrak{g})=-\frac{1}{2n}{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\mathfrak{g}+\nabla^{2}_{\mathfrak{g}}u_{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}e^{-u_{\mathfrak{g}}}.

Since the Poincaré-Einstein metric
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
is a critical point for the entropy, we have
\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}})=0
. The required
L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
estimate follows from Proposition
6.1
.

Condition 2:

For every
\mathfrak{h}\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
the map
\mathfrak{g}\mapsto D_{\mathfrak{g}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\mathfrak{h}):\mathcal{U}\to L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
is continuous.

This follows immediately from the second (Lipschitz) estimate of Proposition
6.1
.

Condition 3:

The linearisation
D_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
is formally self-adjoint, and its restriction as a map
T_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\to T_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathcal{S}^{0}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
is continuous and Fredholm.

A simple computation shows that the linearisation at
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
agrees with the Hessian of Section
4.2
, i.e. (
4.24
):

D_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}=-\frac{1}{2n}{\left(\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}-\delta^{*}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}+\nabla^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(u^{\prime}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}\right)}\right)}.

As the Hessian of a Riemannian functional, it is automatically formally self-adjoint. The restriction to
\mathfrak{h}\in T_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\subset\ker\delta_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
has
u^{\prime}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}-\frac{1}{2}\tr_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}=0
(see (
4.16
)), which leaves only

D_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}|_{T_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathcal{S}^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}=-\frac{1}{4n}\Delta_{E,\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}.

Since
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
is a Poincaré–Einstein metric,
[
KY
, Remark 2.11]
tells us the operator is indeed Fredholm. Continuity follows directly from ellipticity, as all elliptic second order differential operators
P
satisfy
\left|\left|P\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{k-2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}\right|\right|_{H^{k}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}
for all
k\geq 2
and some constant
C>0
.

Condition 4:

The linearisation
D_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}:T_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathcal{S}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\to T_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathcal{S}^{\ell-2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
is continuous and Fredholm.

Fredholmness follows from
[
KY
, Remark 2.11]
(see also
[
Lee
, Proposition D]
). Continuity again follows from ellipticity.

Conditions 1 and 2 show that
\mathfrak{g}\mapsto\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}(\mathfrak{g})
is a
C^{1}
map in a neighbourhood of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
, while Conditions 3 and 4 imply that its derivative at
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
is an isomorphism of the tangent space. Teed up for the use of the inverse function theorem, we can apply
[
KY
, Theorem 5.1]
to complete the proof of the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequalities (
6.11
)-(
6.12
).
∎

6.2
Proofs of Theorems
1.9
and
1.10

We are now finally able to prove our main stability and instability results. However, before presenting the proofs, we need to recall some short time existence and regularity results due to Bamler (see
[
Ba2
]
) that will be useful. In particular, our proofs will be very similar to those in
[
KY
]
, since instead of pairs
{\left(g,\varphi\right)}
we can consider the auxiliary metric
\mathfrak{g}=e^{-2\varphi}d\tau^{2}+g
.

As every static pair
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
on
M
yields a PE metric
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
on
\mathfrak{M}
, the problem reduces to proving stability of PE metrics (as done in
[
KY
]
) with a warped product structure. Importantly, metrics which are warped products remain so when evolving by Ricci flow.

Throughout the proofs of the results in this section, we will mainly consider the auxiliary Ricci-DeTurck flow:

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\mathfrak{g}(t)=-2{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}+n\mathfrak{g}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}_{X_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}(t)\right)}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}(t)\right)}\right)}\\
\mathfrak{g}{\left(0\right)}=\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}.\end{cases}

(6.13)

Here
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}={\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
is a static background metric, and
X_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}
is the Bianchi vector field,

X_{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\mathfrak{h}\right)}:={\left(\beta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right)}^{\sharp}={\left(\delta_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}\right)}^{\sharp}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{\mathfrak{g}}\tr_{\mathfrak{g}}\mathfrak{h}.

We refer the reader to
[
J
, Section 5]
for more on the Ricci-DeTurck flow, and the connection between
X_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
and the usual DeTurck vector field.

Note that, neglecting the term
\mathscr{L}_{X_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}(t)\right)}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}(t)\right)}
, the system (
6.13
) is equivalent to

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}g(t)=-2{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{g(t)}+ng(t)-d\varphi(t)\otimes d\varphi(t)+\nabla^{2}\varphi(t)\right)}\\
\partial_{t}\varphi(t)=-{\left(\Delta_{g(t)}\varphi(t)+\left|\nabla\varphi(t)\right|^{2}-n\right)}\\
{\left(g{\left(0\right)},\varphi{\left(0\right)}\right)}={\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}.\end{cases}

This follows from the formulae of Section
2.15
.

Short time existence, as well as
L^{\infty}
-bounds on the perturbation
\mathfrak{h}(t)=\mathfrak{g}(t)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
, hold due to the following result (
[
Ba2
, Proposition 2.4]
):

Proposition 6.14
.

Let
{\left(\mathfrak{M},\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}
be a complete Riemannian manifold with its curvature tensor globally bounded in the
C^{0,\alpha}
-sense and let
\mathfrak{g}_{0}=\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}+\mathfrak{h}_{0}
be a smooth metric on
\mathfrak{M}
. Then there are constants
C,\varepsilon,\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}>0
, depending only on
\mathfrak{M}
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
, such that, if
\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon
, there is a unique
L^{\infty}
-bounded smooth solution
\left\{\mathfrak{h}(t)\right\}_{t\in\left[0,2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right]}
to (
6.13
). Moreover, we have

\sup_{t\in\left[0,2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right]}\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}(t)\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}}\leq C\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}}.

We are also able to get
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-control, as well as
C^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-control, of
\mathfrak{h}(t)
for
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
as large as we like. This is due to the following result (
[
Ba2
, Lemma 6.2]
):

Lemma 6.15
.

Let
{\left(\mathfrak{M},\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}
be a PE manifold of class
C^{2,\alpha}
, let
\mathfrak{g}_{0}=\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}+\mathfrak{h}_{0}
be a smooth metric on
\mathfrak{M}
and denote the Ricci-DeTurck flow starting from
\mathfrak{h}_{0}
by
\left\{\mathfrak{h}_{t}\right\}_{t\in\left[0,2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right]}
, where
2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
is the existence time from Proposition
6.14
. For every
\ell,b\geq 0
there are constants
A_{\ell},\varepsilon_{\ell}>0
such that, if
\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}}<\varepsilon_{\ell}
and
\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}_{0}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq b
, then

\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}(t)\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq A_{0}b,\qquad\qquad\text{for all }\quad t\in[0,2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}]

and, for
\ell\geq 1
,

\left|\left|\mathfrak{h}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq A_{\ell}b,\qquad\qquad\text{for all }\quad t\in[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}].

Additionally, for all
\ell\geq 0
, we have the pointwise estimate

\left|\nabla^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\mathfrak{h}(t)\right|_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\leq A_{\ell}b,\qquad\qquad\text{for all }\quad t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right].

We are now ready to prove our stability result (Theorem
1.9
), which we restate here in an expanded form for the reader’s convenience. In the expanded statement, we shall use the notion of an
auxiliary neighbourhood
to mean a subset of the set of pairs
(g,\varphi)
, as opposed to a true neighbourhood in the space of all
(n-1)
-dimensional metrics.

Theorem 6.16
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple of class
C^{\ell,\alpha}
(
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
) and dimension
n\geq 3
, and set
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}:=e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}d\tau^{2}+\widehat{g}
. If
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
is a local maximiser of the entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}=\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}
, then for any auxiliary
\big(L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\cap L^{\infty}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\big)
-neighbourhood
\mathcal{U}
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
there exists an auxiliary
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-neighbourhood
\mathcal{V}\subset\mathcal{U}
of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
with the following property:

Any Ricci flow
\mathfrak{g}(t)={\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)}
starting from some
\mathfrak{g}_{0}={\left(g_{0},\varphi_{0}\right)}\in\mathcal{V}
exists for all time, and there is a family of diffeomorphisms
\Phi_{t}
such that
\Phi^{\ast}_{t}\mathfrak{g}(t)={\left(\Phi_{t}^{*}g(t),\Phi^{\ast}_{t}\varphi(t)\right)}
stays in
\mathcal{U}
for all time, and converges in all derivatives to a static pair
{\left(g_{\infty},\varphi_{\infty}\right)}=\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}
as
t\rightarrow\infty
. Moreover, the convergence is polynomial; that is, for all
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
, there exist constants
C,\beta>0
such that

\left|\left|{\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)}-{\left(g_{\infty},\varphi_{\infty}\right)}\right|\right|_{C^{\ell}}\leq C{\left(t-\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}^{-\beta}

for all
t>\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
, where
\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
is (half) the existence time from Proposition
6.14
.

Proof.

For this proof, we shall require the definition of balls centred at
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
with respect to several different metrics. Let

\mathcal{B}^{k}{\left(r\right)}:=\left\{\mathfrak{g}=(g,\varphi)\in H^{k}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(S^{2}\mathfrak{M}\right)}\;;\;\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{k}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}<r\right\}.

With a very slight abuse of notation, we let
\mathcal{B}^{\infty}(r)
denote the analogous ball with respect to the
L^{\infty}
-norm. Let
C,\varepsilon,\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}>0
be the constants from Proposition
6.14
, and assume without loss of generality that
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{B}^{0}(\varepsilon)\cap\mathcal{B}^{\infty}(\varepsilon)
. Let
A_{0}
,
A_{\ell}
and
\varepsilon_{\ell}
be as in Lemma
6.15
, and choose
0<\delta<\varepsilon/A_{0}
so small that

1.

The Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality (Theorem
6.10
) holds on
B^{\ell}(\delta)
.

2.

For all
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta)
,
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}=0
.

3.

The closure of
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta)
is contained in
\mathcal{B}^{\infty}{\left(\min\left\{\varepsilon/C,\varepsilon_{\ell},\varepsilon\right\}\right)}
.

Point 3. is possible since
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
embeds continuously in
L^{\infty}
, and it ensures the existence of the Ricci-DeTurck flow (
6.13
) starting at an arbitrary metric
\mathfrak{g}_{0}\in\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta)
. Furthermore, by design, the flow stays in
\mathcal{U}
for
t\in[0,2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}]
. Recall that the entropy is analytic on
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
, so we can find a
C_{\mu}>0
such that
\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}\right)}\right|\leq C_{\mu}\left|\left|\mathfrak{g}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}
for all
\mathfrak{g}\in\mathcal{B}^{\ell}{\left(\delta\right)}
.

Let
0<\delta^{\prime}<\delta/2A^{*}_{\ell}
be some even smaller radius to be determined later. Here
A_{\ell}^{*}=\max\left\{A_{\ell},1\right\}
ensures that a Ricci-DeTurck flow starting in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta^{\prime})
is contained in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta)
for all
t\in[0,2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}]
and in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}{\left(\delta/2\right)}
for
t\in[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}]
. Specifically, since any such Ricci-DeTurck flow is in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}{\left(\delta/2\right)}
at time
t=2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
, there is some
T=T(\delta^{\prime})>2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
such that any Ricci-DeTurck flow starting in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta^{\prime})
is contained in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta)
for all
t\in[0,T)
.

By the definition of
T
, we have

\sup_{\mathfrak{M}}\left|\mathrm{Rm}_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}\right|_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}\leq C_{0}

for all
t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)
. Shi estimates from
[
S
]
yield bounds on all derivatives of the curvature along an ordinary Ricci flow, while Lemma
6.15
tells us the DeTurck diffeomorphism is as regular as we like. This implies that, for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
, there is a constant
C_{m}
depending only on
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
and
m
such that

\sup_{\mathfrak{M}}\left|\nabla^{m}_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}\mathrm{Rm}_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}\right|_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}\leq C_{m}

(6.17)

for all
t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)
.

We now turn to controlling the derivatives of
u(t):=f_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}-\varphi(t)
. By Lemma
6.15
, the family of metrics
\mathfrak{g}(t)
is contained in an
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-neighbourhood of
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
for all
t\in[0,T)
, and by Proposition
3.27
,
u(t)\in H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}(\mathfrak{M})
. By the Sobolev embedding theorem we even have
u(t)\in C^{2,\alpha}{\left(\mathfrak{M}\right)}
. For the higher order derivatives, we consider only
t\in[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T)
.

We want to bound the
C^{m}
-norm of
u{\left(t\right)}
for all
m
, but since the metrics we are considering are of class
C^{\ell,\alpha}
, Proposition
2.27
will only get us so far. We shall instead use
[
KY
, Corollary 2.10]
. In the following we have suppressed the time dependence to simplify the notation. Recall that
u=u(t)
satisfies the Euler–Lagrange equation (
5.10
):

{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}u=\frac{1}{2}{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n{\left(n+1\right)}\right)}-\frac{1}{2}\left|du\right|^{2}.

where
\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{g}(t)
. Since (
6.17
) provides uniform bounds on the curvature and its derivatives, we can use
[
KY
, Corollary 2.10]
to ensure that
\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n:C^{m,\alpha}\rightarrow C^{m-2,\alpha}
is an isomorphism for all
m\geq 2
, which allow us to compute as follows:

\displaystyle\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{3,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C\left|\left|{\left(\Delta_{\mathfrak{g}}+n\right)}u\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq C{\left(\left|\left|\mathrm{scal}_{\mathfrak{g}}+n{\left(n+1\right)}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}}+\left|\left|\left|du_{g}\right|^{2}\right|\right|_{C^{1,\alpha}}\right)}

\displaystyle<\infty,

as
\left|\left|u\right|\right|_{C^{2,\alpha}}<\infty
and (
6.17
) implies derivatives of the curvature are bounded for all
t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)
. By bootstrapping we can deduce
C^{m,\alpha}
-bounds on
u
for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
. In particular:

\sup_{\mathfrak{M}}\left|\nabla^{m}_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}u(t)\right|_{\mathfrak{g}(t)}\leq\overline{C}_{m}

(6.18)

for all
m\in\mathbb{N}
and all
t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)
, where
\overline{C}_{m}
depends only on
m
and
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
. Note that the estimates (
6.17
) and (
6.18
) are both diffeomorphism invariant.

Now we modify the flow for
t\geq\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
to be more suited for the use of the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality of Theorem
6.10
. For this, consider a Ricci-DeTurck flow
\mathfrak{g}(t)
starting in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta^{\prime})
and the family of diffeomorphisms
\left\{\Phi_{t}\right\}_{t\in[0,T)}
generated by
X_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}(t)\right)}
, and satisfying
\Phi_{0}=\mathrm{Id}
. Add to this the family of diffeomorphisms
\left\{\Psi_{t}\right\}_{t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)}
generated by
-\nabla_{\Phi^{\ast}_{t}\mathfrak{g}(t)}u_{\Phi^{\ast}_{t}\mathfrak{g}(t)}
and satisfying the initial condition
\Psi_{\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}}=\Phi_{\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}}^{-1}
. Now consider the continuous flow starting in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta^{\prime})
, defined by

\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t):=\begin{cases}\mathfrak{g}(t)\penalty\ \penalty\ &\mathrm{for}\penalty\ t\in\left[0,\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right]\\
{\left(\Phi_{t}\circ\Psi_{t}\right)}^{\ast}\mathfrak{g}(t)\penalty\ \penalty\ &\mathrm{for}\penalty\ t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right).\end{cases}

By possibly decreasing it slightly, we may assume that
T
is in fact the maximal time such that this new flow exists and stays within
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta)
for all
t\in[0,T)
, and all initial metrics in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta^{\prime})
. This yields a
modified
Ricci flow

\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)=\begin{cases}-2{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)}+n\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)+\frac{1}{2}\mathscr{L}_{X_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}\penalty\ \penalty\ &\mathrm{for}\penalty\ t\in\left[0,\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)\\
-2{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)}+n\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)+\nabla^{2}_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)}u_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)}\right)}\penalty\ \penalty\ &\mathrm{for}\penalty\ t\in{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)}.\end{cases}

In particular, (
6.18
) and the definition of
T
allow us to replicate the arguments in
[
Ba1
,
Ba2
]
to deduce that Lemma
6.15
still holds for the modified flow and all
t\in[0,T)
.

Since
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)
has the same warped product structure as the initial metric, we can write its component-wise flow for
t\in{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)}
as

\begin{cases}\partial_{t}\widetilde{g}(t)=-2{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\widetilde{g}(t)}+n\widetilde{g}(t)+\nabla^{2}_{\widetilde{g}(t)}f_{\widetilde{g}(t)}-d\widetilde{\varphi}(t)\otimes d\widetilde{\varphi}(t)\right)}\\
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\varphi}(t)=-{\left(\Delta_{\widetilde{g}(t)}\widetilde{\varphi}(t)+\left\langle df_{\widetilde{g}(t)},d\widetilde{\varphi}(t)\right\rangle-n\right)}.\end{cases}

(6.19)

We note that, importantly, this is (
4.4
) – the gradient flow of the entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
.

Moving on, we can use Corollary
A.15
and (
2.20
) to get, for every small
\eta>0
, an
\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
with
\ell^{\prime}>\ell
and constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
, depending on
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}},\ell
and
\ell^{\prime}
, such that for
t\in{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},T\right)}

\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C_{1}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|^{\eta}_{H^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|^{1-\eta}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C_{2}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|^{1-\eta}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

(6.20)

The second inequality and use of Theorem
A.15
are justified by Lemma
6.15
. Next, let
\theta
be the exponent from Theorem
6.10
and choose
\eta>0
such that
\sigma:=\frac{\theta}{2}-\eta+\frac{\theta\eta}{2}>0
. Note that
1-\sigma={\left(1-\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\left(1+\eta\right)}
. Then (
6.20
) and the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality imply that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}

\displaystyle\geq C_{4}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|^{1+\eta}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|^{1-\eta}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{4}}{C_{2}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|^{1+\eta}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{C_{4}}{C_{2}\sqrt{C_{3}}}\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{{\left(1-\frac{\theta}{2}\right)}{\left(1+\eta\right)}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle=:\widetilde{C}\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{1-\sigma}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

where
C_{3}
is the constant from (
6.12
), while the first inequality and constant
C_{4}
comes from
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)
satisfying (
6.19
), albeit with respect to a differently weighted but easily comparable
L^{2}
-norm. We shall keep the vanishing entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}=0
throughout the proof, as it appears as such in the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality. Since all the points from the definition of
\delta
hold along the flow, we have
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}=0
for
t\in[0,T)
, and we may deduce from the inequality above that

\displaystyle-\frac{d}{dt}\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{\sigma}

\displaystyle=\sigma\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{\sigma-1}\frac{d}{dt}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\sigma\widetilde{C}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

Integrating this from
\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
to
T
yields

\int^{T}_{\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}dt\leq\frac{1}{\sigma\widetilde{C}}\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{\sigma}\leq\frac{C_{\mu}}{\sigma\widetilde{C}}\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}^{\sigma},

(6.21)

where the second inequality is again a consequence of the choice of
\delta
. This gives the bound

\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(T)-\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}})\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{g}}}\leq\int^{T}_{\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}dt\leq\frac{C_{\mu}}{\sigma\widetilde{C}}\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}^{\sigma}.

We are finally in a position to specify
\delta^{\prime}
further. Choose it so small that
A_{\ell}\delta^{\prime}+\frac{C_{\mu}}{\sigma\widetilde{C}}{\left(A_{\ell}\delta^{\prime}\right)}^{\sigma}<\delta
. Then, for every initial metric in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}{\left(\delta^{\prime}\right)}
we have
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}\in\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(A_{\ell}\delta^{\prime})
, and

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(T)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}})-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(T)-\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}})\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}})-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+\frac{C_{\mu}}{\sigma\widetilde{C}}\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}})-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle<\delta.

This implies that no flow starting in
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta^{\prime})
leaves
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\delta)
at time
t=T
, and it can not be maximal in the sense defined. The only logical conclusion is that
T=\infty
, which then implies
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\rightarrow\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}
for
t\to\infty
and some limit metric
\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}\in\mathcal{U}
.

Using the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality again, one can compute that, for
t>\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
,

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dt}\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{\theta-1}

\displaystyle=(1-\theta)\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{\theta-2}\frac{d}{dt}\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1-\theta}{C_{3}}\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}^{-2}{\left(\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)},\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right)}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\geq\widehat{C},

for some constant
\widehat{C}>0
. The first inequality uses the Łojasiewicz-Simon inequality and comparability of the volume forms, while the second uses that
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)
agrees with the gradient flow up to a factor
e^{-u_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)}}
. Integrating this from
t=\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
to
T
yields

\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(T)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|\leq\widehat{C}{\left(T-\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}^{-\frac{1}{1-\theta}}.

(6.22)

It is important to note that we may assume
\theta<1
, as the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequaltiy still holds after decreasing the exponent
\theta
, as long as the difference of the entropies is at most
1
. Sending
t\rightarrow\infty
tells us
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}\right)}=\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}
. Therefore, since
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
is a local maximum of the entropy, so too is
\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}
. We may then use
[
DKM
, Corollary 5.15]
to deduce that
\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}
must be a Poincaré–Einstein metric. Moreover, since warped product structure is preserved by the modified Ricci flow, we know that
\mathfrak{g}_{\infty}={\left(g_{\infty},\varphi_{\infty}\right)}
is a static pair.

Finally, combining (
6.21
) and (
6.22
) yields the claimed convergence rate:

\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(T)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq\frac{1}{\sigma\widetilde{C}}\left|\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(T)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right|^{\sigma}\leq\frac{\widehat{C}^{\sigma}}{\sigma\widetilde{C}}{\left(T-\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}^{-\frac{\sigma}{1-\theta}}.

Using Lemma
6.15
and the Sobolev embedding theorem yields the desired convergence in all derivatives. Note also that by diffeomorphism invariance the above results for the modified Ricci flow hold for the standard Ricci flow

\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{RF}}(t):=\begin{cases}{\left(\Phi_{t}\right)}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)&\text{for }t\in[0,\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}]\\
{\left(\Phi_{t}\circ\Psi_{t}\circ\Phi_{t}\right)}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}{\left(t\right)}&\text{for }t\in[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},\infty)\end{cases},

satisfying
\partial_{t}\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{RF}}(t)=-2{\left(\mathrm{Ric}_{\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{RF}}(t)}+n\mathfrak{g}_{\mathrm{RF}}(t)\right)}
for all
t\geq 0
.
∎

Now for the instability theorem (Theorem
1.10
), which we restate here in an expanded form.

Theorem 6.23
.

Let
{\left(M,\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
be a static triple of class
C^{\ell,\alpha}
(
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
) and dimension
n\geq 3
, and set
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}:=e^{-2\widehat{\varphi}}\;d\tau^{2}+\widehat{g}
. Assume that
{\left(\widehat{g},\widehat{\varphi}\right)}
is
not
a local maximiser of the entropy
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}=\mu_{\mathrm{AH},\widehat{g}}
. Then there exists a non-trivial ancient Ricci flow
\left\{\mathfrak{g}(t)\right\}_{t\in(-\infty,0]}=\left\{{\left(g(t),\varphi(t)\right)}\right\}_{t\in(-\infty,0]}
and a family of diffeomorphisms
\left\{\Phi_{t}\right\}_{t\in(-\infty,0]}
, such that
\Phi_{t}^{*}\mathfrak{g}(t)\to\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
in all derivatives as
t\rightarrow-\infty
.

Proof.

Consider
\left\{{\left(g_{i},\varphi_{i}\right)}\right\}=\left\{\mathfrak{g}_{i}\right\}\subset\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}{\left(\mathfrak{M},\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}
, a sequence of smooth metrics on
\mathfrak{M}=\mathbb{S}^{1}\times M
such that
\mathfrak{g}_{i}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
in the
\big(L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}\cap L^{\infty}\big)
-sense. Furthermore, suppose
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}_{i}\right)}>\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}=0
for all
i
, and let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)
be the modified Ricci flow starting at
\mathfrak{g}_{i}
, as defined during the proof of Theorem
6.16
. Importantly, since
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}
is monotonically increasing under the flow, we have

\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right)}=\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}>0

for all
t\geq 0
. Furthermore, for
t\in[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}]

\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\overset{L^{\infty}}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\qquad\text{for}\qquad i\to\infty

(6.24)

by Lemma
6.15
. The same convergence is true in the
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-norm.

Now, take
\mathcal{U}=\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(2\varepsilon)
with
\varepsilon>0
small enough that Theorem
6.10
holds on
\mathcal{U}
. For large enough
i
, convergence implies
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\in\mathcal{U}
for all
t\in[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}},2\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}]
. For these
i
and
t
, we can apply the Łojasiewicz–Simon inequality to deduce that

\frac{d}{dt}{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\theta-1}\geq-C_{\theta},

where
\theta
is the exponent from (
6.12
) and
C_{\theta}=\frac{1-\theta}{C}\geq 0
. Integrating this inequality in time from
t=s
to
T
, we find that

\left[{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(s)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\theta-1}-C_{\theta}{\left(T-s\right)}\right]^{-\frac{1}{1-\theta}}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T\right)}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)},

(6.25)

provided
s
is such that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(s\right)}\in\mathcal{U}
. Let
T_{i}
be the first time that the flow
\widetilde{g}_{i}(t)
leaves
\mathcal{B}^{\ell}(\varepsilon)
; that is, the first time at which

\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}=\varepsilon.

Claim 6.26
.

We have
T_{i}\rightarrow\infty
as
i\rightarrow\infty
.

Proof of claim.

Assume for contradiction that
T_{i}
(possibly after passing to a subsequence) is a bounded sequence, with
T_{\infty}=\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}T_{i}
. Using Corollary
A.15
like before, we have, for any given
\eta>0
, that for
t\in[0,T_{i}]

\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C_{1}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right|\right|^{\eta}_{H^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right|\right|^{1-\eta}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq C_{2}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right|\right|^{1-\eta}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

for some
\ell<\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}
, and constants
C_{1},C_{2}>0
depending only on
\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
,
\ell
and
\ell^{\prime}
. Note that the second inequality and the application of Theorem
A.15
are justified by Lemma
6.15
, which implies the
H^{\ell^{\prime}}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-norm of
\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)
is finite as long as the flow stays in
\mathcal{U}
. Let
\eta>0
be small enough so that
\sigma:=\frac{\theta}{2}-\eta+\frac{\theta\eta}{2}>0
. Then we can compute as we did in the proof of Theorem
6.16
to see that

\frac{d}{dt}{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\sigma}\geq C_{\sigma}\left|\left|\partial_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}.

(6.27)

Integrating this new bound with respect to time from
\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
to
T_{i}
allows us to deduce that

\varepsilon=\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+\frac{1}{C_{\sigma}}{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\sigma}.

(6.28)

Note that (
6.28
) does not rely on
T_{i}
(or the limit,
T_{\infty}
) being finite. Additionally, by (
6.24
) we can make
\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}
as small as we want by taking
i\gg 1
. Using this and rearranging (
6.28
), we obtain the uniform lower bound

\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}\right)},

(6.29)

for some
\varepsilon^{\prime}>0
. Hence, letting
i\rightarrow\infty
, we have
\varepsilon^{\prime}\leq\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{\infty}{\left(T_{\infty}\right)}\right)}
. On the other hand, by (
6.24
), we know that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
in the
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
-norm. Thus
\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{C^{0}}\rightarrow 0
by the Sobolev embedding theorem. Since the limit here is unique, we have
\lim_{i\rightarrow\infty}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}=\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
, which contradicts the uniformly positive lower entropy bound (
6.29
). We must therefore have
\lim_{i\to\infty}T_{i}=\infty
.
∎

We now define a new sequence of metrics by applying a time shift. Let
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}(t):=\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(t+T_{i}\right)}
for
t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}-T_{i},0\right]
. Then, by the previous part of the proof, we have

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}(t)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq\varepsilon\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for}\;\;t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}-T_{i},0\right],
