\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}-T_{i}\right)}\overset{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}{\longrightarrow}\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\qquad\qquad

\displaystyle\text{for}\;\;i\to\infty.

Additionally, note that we can apply Hamilton’s compactness theorem (see
[
H1
]
) to pass to a convergent subsequence, so that
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}(t)\rightarrow\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)
in
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
as
i\to\infty
, where
\left\{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\right\}_{t\in(-\infty,0]}=\left\{{\left(\widetilde{g}(t),\widetilde{\varphi}(t)\right)}\right\}_{t\in(-\infty,0]}
is an ancient modified Ricci flow satisfying the system (
6.19
).

Next, for
t\in(-\infty,0]
, let
\left\{\Psi_{t}\right\}_{t\in(-\infty,0]}
be the family of diffeomorphisms generated by
-\nabla_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)}u_{\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)}
. Then
\mathfrak{g}(t):=\Psi^{\ast}_{t}\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)
is a Ricci flow. Furthermore, sending
i\to\infty
in (
6.28
), we have

\varepsilon\leq\frac{1}{C_{\sigma}}{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}(0)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\sigma}\leq\left|\left|\nabla\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\mathfrak{g}(0)\right)}\right|\right|^{\frac{2\sigma}{2-\theta}}_{L^{2}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

where the second inequality is due Theorem
6.10
. Note that
\mathfrak{g}(0)
appears since
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}(0)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}
, and the latter is what is present in (
6.28
). This, together with the monotonicity of the entropy (Lemma
4.13
), implies that
\mathfrak{g}(t)
is a non-trivial Ricci flow. That is, it is not a stationary solution of (
4.4
); hence, by diffeomorphism invariance, it is not a stationary solution of (
1.3
).

By integrating (
6.27
) from
\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}
to
t+T_{i}
, and applying (
6.25
) (with
s=t+T_{i}
and
T=T_{i}
), we find that, for
t\in\left[\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}-T_{i},0\right]
and
i\gg 1
,

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}-T_{i}\right)}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle=\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}-\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(t+T_{i}\right)}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{\sigma}}\int_{\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}}^{t+T_{i}}\frac{d}{ds}{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}(s)\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\sigma}\;ds

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{\sigma}}{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(t+T_{i}\right)}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\sigma}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{C_{\sigma}}{\left(-C_{\theta}t+{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\theta-1}\right)}^{-\frac{\sigma}{1-\theta}}

\displaystyle\leq{\left(-C_{3}t+C_{4}\right)}^{-\frac{\sigma}{1-\theta}},

for some
C_{3},C_{4}>0
. Note that the equality is due to the definition of
\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}
, while the second inequality relies on
\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}\right)}\right)}>0
, and the last inequality is due to (
6.29
) and
\theta<1
, so that

{\left(\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}_{i}{\left(T_{i}\right)}\right)}-\mu_{\mathrm{AH}}{\left(\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right)}\right)}^{\theta-1}\leq{\left(\varepsilon^{\prime}\right)}^{1-\theta}.

The triangle inequality now yields

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)-\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}(t)\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}+{\left(-C_{4}t+C_{5}\right)}^{-\frac{\sigma}{1-\theta}}+\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}^{*}_{i}{\left(\tau_{\mathrm{SE}}-T_{i}\right)}-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}},

and by letting
i\to\infty
, it reduces to

\displaystyle\left|\left|\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)-\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}\right|\right|_{H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}}\leq{\left(-C_{4}t+C_{5}\right)}^{-\frac{\sigma}{1-\theta}}.

Finally, sending
t\rightarrow-\infty
we have
{\left(\Psi_{t}\right)}_{\ast}\mathfrak{g}(t)=\widetilde{\mathfrak{g}}(t)\rightarrow\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}
in
H^{\ell}_{\widehat{\mathfrak{g}}}
. Convergence in all derivatives follows from Lemma
6.15
and the Sobolev embedding theorem.
∎

Appendix A
Appendix

This appendix is devoted to recording some variational formulas we use throughout the paper. The proofs can be found in, say,
[
Be
]
.

Proposition A.1
.

Let
\left(M,g\right)
be a Riemannian manifold. Then, with
g_{s}=g+sh
for
\left|s\right|\ll 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathrm{Ric}_{g_{s}}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\Delta_{L}h-\delta^{*}_{g}\delta_{g}h-\frac{1}{2}\nabla^{2}_{g}\tr_{g}\left(h\right),

(A.2)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\mathrm{scal}_{g_{s}}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{g}\tr_{g}\left(h\right)+\delta_{g}\delta_{g}h-\left<\mathrm{Ric}_{g},h\right>.

(A.3)

Proposition A.4
.

Let
\left(M,g\right)
be a Riemannian manifold,
f,v,\varphi,\psi\in C^{1}{\left(M\right)}
, and
h
a symmetric
2
-tensor on
M
. Then, with
g_{s}=g+sh
(for
\left|s\right|\ll 1
),
f_{s}=f+sv
, and
\varphi_{s}=\varphi+s\psi
, we have

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}e^{-f_{s}}dV_{g_{s}}

\displaystyle=\left(-v+\frac{1}{2}\tr_{g}\left(h\right)\right)e^{-f}dV_{g},

(A.5)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\nabla^{2}_{g_{s}}f_{s}

\displaystyle=\nabla^{2}_{g}v-\delta^{*}_{g}{\left(h\cdot df\right)}+\frac{1}{2}h\times\nabla^{2}_{g}f+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{g,\nabla_{g}f}h,

(A.6)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\Delta_{g_{s}}\varphi_{s}

\displaystyle=\Delta_{g}\psi+\left\langle h,\nabla^{2}_{g}\varphi\right\rangle-\left\langle\delta_{g}h+\frac{1}{2}d\tr_{g}h,d\varphi\right\rangle,

(A.7)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\left\langle d\varphi_{s},df_{s}\right\rangle_{g_{s}}

\displaystyle=-\left\langle h,df\otimes d\varphi\right\rangle_{g}+\left\langle dv,d\varphi\right\rangle_{g}+\left\langle df,d\psi\right\rangle_{g}.

(A.8)

Here
\times
denotes the symmetrised contraction
{\left(h\times k\right)}_{ij}=g^{lm}{\left(h_{il}k_{mj}+h_{jl}k_{mi}\right)}
for symmetric
2
-tensors
h,k
.

Note that (
A.6
) is written in a slightly non-standard way compared to the version in
[
Be
]
. However, it is equivalent and is convenient for our purposes.

Proposition A.9
.

Let
h
be a symmetric
2
-tensor on a Riemannian manifold
\left(M,g\right)
. Further let
\omega
be a
1
-form on
M
and write
\omega_{s}=\omega+s\omega^{\prime}
. Then

\displaystyle\left(\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\delta_{g_{s}}h\right)

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}\delta_{g}\left(h\times h\right)-\frac{1}{2}h\cdot d\tr_{g}\left(h\right)+\frac{1}{4}d\left|h\right|^{2}_{g}

(A.10)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\delta_{g_{s}}\omega_{s}

\displaystyle=-\left(g^{ij}\nabla_{i}\omega_{i}\right)^{\prime}=\delta_{g}\omega^{\prime}+\frac{1}{2}g^{ij}\left(2\nabla_{i}h_{j\ell}-\nabla_{\ell}h_{ij}\right)\omega^{\ell}+\left<h,\nabla_{g}\omega\right>

(A.11)

\displaystyle\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\left(\delta_{g_{s}}\delta_{g_{s}}h\right)

\displaystyle=\delta_{g}\left(\left.\frac{d}{ds}\right|_{s=0}\delta_{g_{s}}h\right)-\left|\delta_{g}h\right|^{2}_{g}-\frac{1}{2}\left<\delta_{g}h,d\tr_{g}\left(h\right)\right>_{g}+\left<h,\nabla_{g}h\right>_{g}

(A.12)

Theorem A.13

(Yamabe Problem)
.

Let
\ell>\frac{n}{2}+2
,
\frac{n}{2}<\beta<n
, and
g\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\frac{1}{2}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}\cap\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
. Then there is a unique
\omega\in H^{\ell}_{\frac{1}{2}}{\left(M\right)}\cap C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}(M)
such that
\widetilde{g}=e^{2\omega}g
has constant scalar curvature
\mathrm{scal}_{\widetilde{g}}=-n(n-1)
.

Proof.

The heavy lifting is done by
[
ALM
, Theorem 1.1/6.8]
, though the right application requires a little finesse. With regards to the assumptions of that theorem, it will only work in our setting when the dimension
n
is odd. In that case
g\in\mathcal{R}^{\ell}_{\frac{1}{2}}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
is of class
\mathscr{H}^{\ell,2;\frac{n+1}{2}}
, and the indices satisfy the requirements of the theorem. However, when
n
is even, the best fortified space we can achieve is
\mathscr{H}^{\ell,2;\frac{n}{2}}
, which is just shy of the requirements. Fortunately, a closer inspection of the proof of
[
ALM
, Theorem 6.8]
reveals that we may bypass the notion of fortified spaces, as in our case
g
satisfies the slightly stronger condition
g-\widehat{g}\in H^{\ell}_{m-\frac{n}{2}}
(and in particular
\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n(n-1)\in H^{\ell-2}_{m-\frac{n}{2}}
) for
m=\frac{n+1}{2}
. The proof proceeds to construct a solution
u\in H^{\ell}_{m-\frac{n}{2}}{\left(M\right)}
to the Yamabe equation

4\frac{n-1}{n-2}\Delta_{g}u+\mathrm{scal}_{g}(1+u)=-n(n-1)(1+u)^{\frac{n+2}{n-2}}.

Setting
\widetilde{g}:={\left(1+u\right)}^{\frac{4}{n-2}}g
, we have found our constant scalar curvature metric, though we still need to check that the conformal factor has the right decay. By defining
\omega:=\frac{2}{n-2}\ln{\left(1+u\right)}
, we obtain another conformal factor in
H^{\ell}_{\frac{1}{2}}{\left(M\right)}
, satisfying the transformed Yamabe equation

{\left(\Delta_{g}+n\right)}\omega=\frac{n-2}{2}\left|d\omega\right|^{2}_{g}-\frac{1}{2(n-1)}{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n(n-1)\right)}-\frac{n}{2}F(\omega),

where
F(\omega)=e^{2\omega}-1-2\omega=\mathcal{O}{\left(\omega^{2}\right)}
. By Lemma
2.14
, we have
\omega\in C^{2,\alpha}_{1/2}{\left(M\right)}
. It follows that
\left|d\omega\right|^{2}_{g}\in C^{1,\alpha}_{1}{\left(M\right)}
and
F(\omega)\in C^{2,\alpha}_{1}{\left(M\right)}
. Since
g\in\mathcal{R}^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M,\widehat{g}\right)}
, and since
\beta>\frac{n}{2}>1
we have

\frac{n-2}{2}\left|d\omega\right|^{2}_{g}-\frac{1}{2(n-1)}{\left(\mathrm{scal}_{g}+n(n-1)\right)}-\frac{n}{2}F(\omega)\in C^{0,\alpha}_{1}{\left(M\right)}.

It follows from Proposition
2.27
, that
\omega\in C^{2,\alpha}_{1}{\left(M\right)}
. We may now bootstrap up to
C^{2,\alpha}_{\beta}{\left(M\right)}
, as in
[
DKM
, Proposition 4.3]
.
∎

Finally, we will need the following version of the Gagliardo–Nirenberg inequality.

Theorem A.14
.

Let
g
be AH of class
C^{\ell,\alpha}
and let
T\in H^{k}_{1/2}(S^{2}M)\cap L^{q}_{1/2}(S^{2}M)
for
k\leq\ell
and
k+\frac{n}{q}\geq\frac{n}{2}
. Then, for every
0\leq i\leq k
, there is a constant
C=C(k,q,n)
such that

\left|\left|\nabla^{i}T\right|\right|_{L^{p}_{1/2}}\leq C\left|\left|\nabla^{k}T\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{1/2}}^{\theta}\left|\left|T\right|\right|_{L^{q}_{1/2}}^{1-\theta}

for every pair
(p,\theta)
such that

\frac{n}{2}-\frac{n}{p}\leq k-i\qquad\&\qquad\theta{\left(k-\frac{n}{2}+\frac{n}{q}\right)}=i-\frac{n}{p}+\frac{n}{q}

and
\frac{i}{k}\leq\theta\leq 1
.

Proof.

By
[
Lee
, Lemma 3.9]
we can approximate
T
by a sequence
{\left(T_{j}\right)}_{j}\subset C^{\infty}_{c}(S^{2}M)
, so
T_{j}\to T
in
H^{k}_{1/2}(S^{2}M)
. By the classical Gagliardo-Nirenberg estimate for tensors (see
[
H2
, Corollary 12.7]
- although it is for compact manifolds, the argument is the same for compactly supported tensors), there is a constant
C>0
depending only on
k,q,n
such that for any pair
(p,\theta)
with

\theta{\left(k-\frac{n}{2}+\frac{n}{q}\right)}=i-\frac{n}{p}+\frac{n}{q}

the following holds:

\left|\left|\nabla^{i}T_{j}\right|\right|_{L^{p}_{1/2}}\leq C\left|\left|\nabla^{k}T_{j}\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{1/2}}^{\theta}\left|\left|T_{j}\right|\right|_{L^{q}_{1/2}}^{1-\theta}.

As
T\in H^{k}_{1/2}(S^{2}M)\cap L^{q}_{1/2}(S^{2}M)
, the norms on the right converge to the corresponding norms of
T
. By the Sobolev embedding theorem (see
[
Lee
, Lemma 3.6(c)]
), the norm on the left converges to the corresponding norm of
T
under the assumption that
\frac{n}{2}-\frac{n}{p}\leq k-i
.
∎

Corollary A.15
.

With assumptions as in Theorem
A.14
, we have

\left|\left|\nabla^{i}T\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\varphi}}\leq C\left|\left|\nabla^{k}T\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\varphi}}^{i/k}\left|\left|T\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\varphi}}^{1-i/k}\qquad 0\leq k\leq\ell

\left|\left|\nabla^{i}T\right|\right|_{L^{2k/i}_{\varphi}}\leq C\left|\left|\nabla^{k}T\right|\right|_{L^{2}_{\varphi}}^{i/k}\left|\left|T\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}}^{1-i/k}\qquad\frac{n}{2}\leq k\leq\ell

where
\varphi\in C^{\ell,\alpha}(M)
is a function with asymptotics
\varphi=-r+\mathcal{O}{\left(e^{-r}\right)}
, with respect to the distance function described in Remark
2.5
. Furthermore,
L^{2}_{\varphi}
denotes the standard
L^{2}
space, with respect to the weighted volume form
e^{-\varphi}\;dV_{g}
.
